# W385 declaration-prefix preflight

Status: source port and policy integration in progress; the child clamp is connected locally, while the ordinary shell policy remains Partial. Base `9c51463`. The existing W373 card is a provisional predicate, not an implementation authorization to guess the rule. Product adaptation under review uses the copied `E6`/`pyr` bodies synchronously with the existing parser. The host launches inherited, unscrubbed environments, so `wd` is false; it has no corresponding feature reader, so `$Ts` uses the observed no-reader default true. The copied `ht` remains `os.homedir()` as in the source, whose unset-HOME result differs from the observed local `/bin/sh` value. These choices do not claim other source configurations. Keep ordinary-permission sampling separate from clamp completeness, and preserve the existing host reading checks until their replacement is source-backed. The child clamp now applies the copied source analyser separately, with a conservative legacy guard when HOME is absent. Verify default, bypass, explicit denial, approvals, hook-updated input and child execution against source-bound cases before marking this package complete.

## Integration design

Keep the recovered analyser's state at module lifetime, as in the source: `E6` synchronously saves and restores its prefix state, while `A`, `j` and `P` remain monotonic counters. This replaces the per-check factory proposed by the scratch prototype and avoids resetting those counters. The local environment adapters set `wd` to false and `$Ts` to its no-reader default true. Retain the source's call-time `os.homedir()` for `ht`; when HOME is absent, this can differ from `/bin/sh`, and the caller must keep any existing incomplete-reading restriction rather than treat the inferred home as proof of the shell's value. A generated source-copy plan records all 146 original ranges and hashes, and the existing parser exports its already-copied `yyr` helper. The planned source result adapter must distinguish parser failure, a `too-complex` result, and a simple command array. It must carry the prefix-retention vector and each `pyr` sample separately. The shared `shellReadings.exhaustive` contract remains with the existing ordinary tree/text reader and the absent-HOME legacy guard; the new sampling completeness belongs only to permission combination. The existing host deny/ask/allow readers, hook-input recheck and approval owners remain in place. A sampled source result must preserve the original whole-line decision when checking the source command texts. An independent reader confirmed that each vector runs the entire `O3e`/`D3e` pipeline, with source early returns, path checks, subcommand checks and an object-sensitive `ior` merge. Treating each `commands[].text` as an ordinary top-level host decision would therefore be an unverified policy rewrite. Product integration remains gated on mapping those branches; the pre-change compiled baselines are recorded below. An owned factory prototype generated from the same 146 hash-bound ranges isolates state per instance; interleaved calls with two HOME values retain the expected old/new readings and return each owner's HOME. The product-shaped plan SHA-256 is `ecdcae6d6615f02712da2ba21586902f82625b4660f8486d0cb0318d0003d938`, generated candidate `7abf968890a43de84edd5f23e80d33bcb09ba761cc44f64c6013abc9ff27142f`, ledger `5e4c1d05a8bb26724fd56978f25b3e1884915a64ef2f4e873c7a36fff1a32563`, and runner `e87db9c35147388033dabe1047068afa88d0f32a69cebad4d1ed3b1c4a43cef1`. All 146 body hashes and text ranges match; the scope scanner reports zero unresolved names after TypeScript transpilation. Running the JavaScript-only scanner directly on a typed parameter failed before analysis; the transpiled candidate is the checked artifact. The prototype uses an exact copied `yyr` helper and the compiled parser. The ordinary per-vector policy remains unconnected.

## Source port

The source analyser and `pyr` exist as a product module. The analyser now serves the child clamp; `pyr` remains unconnected to ordinary authorization. Its transpiled dependency scan finds zero unresolved identifiers. `research/ports/W385-shell-analyser-source-plan.json` regenerates `src/shell-analyser-source.ts` and its ledger through `scripts/copy-source-plan.mjs`. The plan SHA-256 is `1ebbee229c4ab77d4372cc872f41608ae34fe99514d3bccd0daf612dcce0ea41`; the generated module SHA-256 is `0f3d9dbc4da0e3ca1aa40af09e9092e2431d3913186cd0459a0e1dee164e01b3`; the ledger SHA-256 is `cbfceb803bb202de3200c3cec4ac2cce388bc923f5d4985a1b6f1fb80d912d1d`. Main independently compared all 146 ledger records, module digests, body hashes, and output slices. The generated wrapper preserves source module-lifetime counters and imports `os.homedir` directly, with documented `wd=false` and `$Ts=true` local adaptations. The copied parser exports its existing `yyr` declaration, and its generator's check passes.

An owned, hash-pinned probe exercises three- and four-prefix enumeration, later `old`/`new` readings, 20 interleaved repetitions, parser-abort identity, and two successive HOME values. A Unicode-prefixed case exposed an incorrect fixture assumption: its false vector returns a source `too-complex` string result while its true vector yields `echo new`; the probe now asserts both observed outcomes rather than assuming every vector is simple. Probe SHA-256 `a78b078091227241017b60a3513e5ea9ca4cb67fcfa5879ade18207373f0fe85`. The source module is not yet connected to authorization, and this probe does not validate the policy chain. `npm run check`, `npm run build`, and the unchanged shell-permission suite (8/8) pass. The compiled six-case CLI driver also passes. Two durable analyser regressions pass after adding a test-only options helper to accommodate TypeScript's inferred signature; the first check failed before that helper, while its runtime tests passed. After adding the merge module and exact vector/message assertions, the resulting identity is `6899977428f8d1a6894308cf85ac8883247e339b31262f04facee1e48cac0099`, with inputs `729d20df21e633eb72265051584e4c04f8a328480d3a82a6fddb56fa10c2e46b`, validation `ae31fb2e4564b3cd39455edacd859173f3bdb2de7ae0ffd830098c82d728dded`, and build `a16263f8b52ba05e4b33c4fdeb8443040662da1d064052e883e09049efc41c47`. Check/build and 11/11 analyser, merge and existing shell-permission tests pass.

## Merge closure

`DTr`, `Lcn`, `jwe`, `m0`, `utt`, `ize`, and `Dl` were selected from `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3234534,3234967), [3234326,3234534), [3234078,3234326), [3354764,3354848), [3354561,3354764), [609639,609790), and [3365554,3365753). `X3` and its original circuit-breaker table are from `chunk-a60ee1ne.js` [882,2303), module SHA-256 `fe5d3ed9cb5d16b40ded156d060aeed714dc3a382405609422688756f0707ee1`; both modules match the extraction manifest. These intervals are zero-based half-open UTF-16 units. A copied, unconnected merge fixture uses the original bodies with an inert local message adapter. It verifies deny precedence, hard-ask preservation, exhaustive winner selection, non-exhaustive soft ask, and source-object identity. Scratch plan SHA-256 `bd51b318f5aeac5d67ee5f6316c90584a1ea5805a897b2e1a89f1ccc1a0e7915`; copied fixture SHA-256 `3510950fa694f5e8be352d819e180923cb902a78a5f5fc5c86705bc486eff5f0`. The durable plan `research/ports/W385-shell-reading-merge-source-plan.json` copies ten precise declarations, including the original `trn` declarator and the original `X3`/`d` functions. Its SHA-256 is `7047dd785dc5ba33d724a8f49814a552500a18d56573aa6d980f6e237820ef1d`; generated module `src/shell-reading-merge-source.ts` is `27d12761e8966e8d17fd23f9cba80f5ab7da22c841400155c25237db1d96823e`; its ledger is `ef36be6fac679b472bbe92f9ac1059f4848d00690d1dcb61b54cf15a610cc532`. Main verified all ten body slices, and the transpiled module has zero unresolved identifiers. The compiled module reproduces the old/new readings and preserves a deny. The source's synthesized `DTr` ask is `type:other`, so the local `Rl` adapter returns that reason string; other `Rl` reason variants are not part of this module's call site. Three durable tests cover the analyser and merge, including a bypass-immune read-block ask retaining its winning object. This proves the bounded merge inputs only; host policy results still need a faithful projection into the source decision shape and a vector-level policy owner.

## Vector policy review

An independent reader verified the source ordering through the `D3e` prechecks, semantics, sandbox, compound-command and multi-directory branches. Those branches precede the final per-command `jcn` check, so projecting every analyser `commands[].text` to a host `decideOnce` is not a source-equivalent implementation. For a conditional example, `cd /tmp; cd /var; rm target` reaches the multiple-directory branch before `jcn`, provided the path and nested-shell checks return no earlier result. Main verified that the copied `E6` returns three simple commands for that input and that `X=1 export Y; cd /tmp; cd /var; rm target` yields the same four commands in both prefix vectors. The host source execution returns deny for both commands in default and bypass modes when `Bash(rm target)` is denied. `Dcn` [3223957,3225417) and `bG` [3105028,3108425) were also inspected; the source rule-store, path and feature premises still need a controlled proof before asserting the source's final verdict. Source `VW`, `chunk-a60ee1ne.js` [4691,5003), body SHA-256 `3d607c8b197353a1d5c1eb7d919aa664c78998d42ff3e111fe77a0c9e958b80f`, returns a bypass-immune `outsideReadsBlocked` safety ask, which the current host `PermissionDecision` shape does not represent. `Agent.authorize` routes an ask without a `floor` into auto classification (`src/agent.ts:587`); a projection that drops the source's `classifierApprovable:false` and safety reason would therefore change that decision path. The final source mode wrapper and Hook recheck also consume the reason, so a safe adapter must preserve those distinctions end to end. Pipeline-to-command mapping and other early-return predicates remain open. A further source detail qualifies the conditional multi-directory example: `O3e` applies `$cn` [3253132,3253708), SHA-256 `724ee7599692796f6c6d2c7aa85f60073db9bb760955e046a46c5ff1be9f1743`, to its ask, and `$cn` rechecks the original line, `Kg` splits, subcommand-result keys and `ost` forms against deny/ask rules. A scratch copy of `Kg` [1072,1477) and its `m`/`E`/`D0` declarators [967,1007)/[1008,1051)/[1052,1058), `chunk-m00pavz3.js` module SHA-256 `e4dcdda42fdf15ff719179977326a414b62ad13d5992832859839c9203c16a62`, splits both candidate lines into their literal subcommands, including `rm target`. With a matching source deny rule and the stated earlier conditions, `$cn` therefore upgrades the multi-directory ask to deny. The independent reader's suggested final ask is refuted for this exact rule; the earlier `D3e` ordering is established but is not by itself a final-verdict counterexample. Copy-plan SHA-256 `954f9c3675893c164c318810b409e4cd875ec6a807210cda94b18b98e7bd1be2`, copied `Kg` fixture SHA-256 `fed031347b4199a2eb7fb85df93fd412f50dcca1f479f953618d8b449968db1d`; it reuses the compiled product parser. A generic deny-first loop still lacks source evidence for all of these branches. The recovered `ior`/`O3e`/`D3e` and their nested checks are asynchronous. Its `OAn` [3104531,3104568), SHA-256 `7de05c7da6d83c61b33f9b26330e93146ead854666d4fc5e3238a747c056a529`, uses an `AsyncLocalStorage` instance to suppress duplicate telemetry during sampled checks; the only other store read in this module is `dO` [3104568,3104621), SHA-256 `134cdfde186177d2a96a3fccba23a7f4bc68bd4ea54292bd22ae7debf6665515`. The no-telemetry host adaptation can omit that sink, but must retain sequential awaited vector evaluation and source result identity. The current `permissionDecision` is synchronous and is also used by synchronous diff-panel readers, so the future adapter needs a distinct async shell authorization boundary or an explicitly justified migration; replacing the shared signature alone would alter unrelated readers. Agent authorization, PermissionRequest updated-input rechecks, PreToolUse reauthorization, skills and child-registry wrappers are already asynchronous callers, while the child clamp is a separate precheck. Source `de`, `chunk-p46ep603.js` [4120,5234), SHA-256 `b36b198420ff43a4f53ccbb2a55e75946883b414595bba38684a341c59b52b88`, derives effective context at call time and overlays allowed/disallowed tools, command clamps, prompt settings, sandbox suspension, mode and working directory. The host's live owner and `effectivePermissionPolicy` must supply equivalent current state across every awaited vector, rather than a cached policy assembled before sampling. The migration inventory also includes the auto-mode post-classification recheck and its pre-hook floor check (`src/agent.ts:602` and `:897`), and the skill executor's independent `approve` wrapper (`src/skill-runtime.ts:74` and `:276`). Interactive skill invocation supplies a once-only confirmation callback (`src/interactive.ts:3867-3875`); headless CLI invocation supplies none (`src/cli.ts:1030-1031`), so both paths must retain their distinct approval behavior. Model-hook and MCP-hook tool checks use the same function but their catalogs are limited to named read-only file tools and read-only namespaced MCP tools respectively (`src/agent.ts:292-346`), and their execution contexts disallow shell; they do not consume the local shell decision path. Source `$pn` and `yk` first parse via `Zwe` [3355651,3356264), SHA-256 `95e29a86fccb34d21a6068f2a631fba82b957a75beca62b96f3bfe365873fc1a`, including a feature-gated unrecognized-key path; the host currently parses `runCommandSchema` at execution (`src/tools.ts:1190`) after `permissionDecision`. Any async migration must specify malformed inputs and cancellation at the permission boundary rather than silently adopting either ordering. These are identified consumers, not a completed migration.

## Policy dependency census

A scope-aware scan of the exact `O3e` and `D3e` bodies reports 88 unique names not declared within those two bodies: 48 module-level functions, seven module-level declarators, and 33 imports, including four Node `path` imports. The scanner input and module were compared against the recorded ranges and extraction-manifest digest before scanning. The census is direct lexical dependency evidence, not a transitive closure or a claim that all branches execute. The locator resolved all 29 extracted-module imports to exact declarations after verifying their module digests; the other four are Node `path` imports. Its scratch JSON has SHA-256 `5fa2f26c21bd43837fde02a0bca400d99718e15852b4d91a493f3234f71edeb0`; the Acorn locator is `408da79ec2600f2c8d5415387de1ea3de697b12c905a8b90fbc0a24c4e3c6072`. Among exact same-module ranges, current ledgers cover `Dl` and `sfn`; imported `D`, `E6`, `F`, `VW`, `Vs`, `X3`, `Ye`, `Yx`, `_`, `_n` and `ie` have copies recorded elsewhere. Coverage records alone do not establish that those copies or their adapters can serve the policy chain. For example, `Vs` is copied inside `src/shell-analyser-source.ts` but is not exported; its availability at the `D3e` boundary still needs an explicit source-plan change and verification. The source parser wrapper `w6`, `chunk-zjewzgfh.js` [1300,1631), SHA-256 `ea6db9ea71d7575b3c49e8da8c83b06e3fbfead22e74b3148cd8c875ca8a585c`, is async and maps empty input to null, length above `$e=10000`, parser null and exceptions to `Yx`; the existing `shellReadings` wrapper has its own contract and must not be substituted without checking those branches. The sandbox decision `Cy`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3287468,3287930), SHA-256 `0eed09e2c0389b0142c761da5fa8eea534403cd77592ed309542d2fb92b7f1a8`, and `Tv` [3213857,3213891), SHA-256 `b81416fda935b8e69b74518a71a7f7b79f79f8c5b4149216093de860308b906d`, derive their values from several runtime settings; the scheduler's fixture callback must continue to provide their combined result. The direct census also locates `Rcn` [3195199,3195390), `Kvr` [3195390,3195877), `MTr` [3230062,3230922), `jcn` [3213895,3215588), `Nwe` [3246934,3247217), and `nfn` [3247217,3251642), all in the same verified module. For read-blocked commands, `jcn` also invokes `Acn` [3202117,3202428) for unsafe environment prefixes and `hTr` [3210785,3213857), SHA-256 `a0efc26f223f44aa0dcf6fa1b3f7f0fb63ae91dc89c48c29e7ac5106d076884c`, for command-specific path and stdin checks before allow. `hTr` uses real path resolution, working directories, glob handling and deny-rule results; the scheduler's `VW` projection alone does not implement this path. Its helpers `PN` [4296,4384), `Wy` [4384,4691) and `arn` [5003,5495) in manifest-verified `chunk-a60ee1ne.js` preserve `outsideReadsBlocked` as a non-classifier-approvable safety result for computed paths or unapproved `sed`. An owned compiled host decision probe with `mode:bypass`, `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories:true` and `Bash(cat:*)` allow returned allow for both `cat /etc/passwd` and `env CUSTOM=value cat /etc/passwd`. It did not execute either command or establish the source's full decision for these inputs. The compiled source analyser returns simple commands and the copied semantics check returns `{ok:true}` for both under its recorded defaults. This observation shows the host's current Shell decision does not, by itself, enforce the source path. `cat` is handled by the source command-path table `b7` [3127489,3133427), `nvr` [3147047,3147392), `icn` [3146099,3146680), and `Zwr` [3141218,3146099), SHA-256 `480415e8ec0f8e30ed2ab2488bb381f33f6cb24ca172e9430eafcaa2e5a50afe`, under `vX`; it is not handled by `hTr`'s generic-argument branch. The shared source path decision `sR`, `chunk-qxmpnv25.js` [941246,942509), SHA-256 `77bae42d45dd0277e272769ea6e630bbb0c192f3ae3f2d435598ee5a6551d247`, delegates a resolved read to `igr` [942509,942809) and `Rk` [934244,936891), SHA-256 `26bf18279d13e7e40a10a4e62cc8ef1689cc1879c90be3c89e1589de02306870`. `Rk` checks deny rules and `uTe` before ordinary working-directory or allow-rule branches. Source `uTe`, `chunk-2bcyxj9q.js` [215177,215388), SHA-256 `ddb8525c16816584efd3efcad9ba7fc9dc44a1c4ba7d1cfe99cb355c4e48b635`, tests the configured read block against the working-directory set and its explicit read-block exception. `uTe` calls `Yg` with `SDn` [215024,215177), which excludes project-settings directories, and calls the read-block exception function `Xce` [242279,243972), SHA-256 `07d6876abc844f5dba33e3b379c13f685e293c745e1446dfdb14f6c440cb4ed5`, in the same module; `Xce` includes selected session, plan and user configuration paths, so a broad workspace-only replacement would miss exceptions. A full source verdict still depends on those path and rule predicates and the host's actual workspace. `Kvr` distinguishes groups from pipelines and uses `qvr` [3195089,3195199) to remove output redirections before `Gvr` [3192162,3195089) awaits each segment's whole `O3e` evaluation in order. `Gvr` then combines their decisions and checks multiple directory changes and Git-sensitive conditions before its aggregate allow. That is a separate recursion path from the host's current flat readings. `MTr` rechecks denial before considering a complex parse; `jcn` checks path and read-block results before its allow returns. `Nwe` calls `nfn` unless a process option disables it or `wX` disables it, and keys its memo by depth, working directory, enclosing command texts and command. `wX` [3104375,3104451) obtains its flag through `$n` in `chunk-bk5ct2gw.js` [528673,528733), module SHA-256 `efbc2d987608904a4a3ad7221f39f6e6afe34c13076d2ce3e4be2d49f43e6dd1`, passing default true. Its `getFeatureValueWithSource` method [158791,159581), SHA-256 `7e668372f36f9cba55696871188e4c69ed441d207c0c9d8d79a6adf22413f58c`, returns that default with source `disabled` or `fallback` when no local override, payload or cached value applies, so `wX` is true in that stated configuration. Only an explicit false payload disables `wX`; the process option is a separate gate. The host search found no direct counterpart for the option or `dangerousRemoval` outside the copied merge table. `nfn` recursively checks shell scripts, tracks prior variable and directory statements, and passes candidate commands to `Fcn` [3243430,3246934), SHA-256 `23f92b2a1385a63a62ae28f9aef036ea6aefffb7821fc7333780c55b81d43a5b`. An unreadable matching script can return `Lwe` [3242762,3243430), SHA-256 `ed18ecf6eea25264a0d7c81be514f8a1820d5b7daa91e9f6b9e9d0b39388c79b`, an ask with `circuitBreaker:dangerousRemoval`; the copied circuit-breaker table marks this bypass-immune. Local activation and transitive dependencies must be established rather than assuming the path inactive. The foreground host uses `/bin/sh` (`src/tools.ts:675`), while the source names and samples Bash retention. A compiled analyser probe for `X=old; X=new export Y; printf %s "$X"` yields sampled `printf %s old` and `printf %s new`; the previously recorded local `/bin/sh` observation outputs `new`. Evaluating every sampled vector would cover that case, but source Bash-retention metadata alone is not a runtime Shell-equivalence claim. Skill invocations can select a different shell, so executor-specific equivalence remains open. A scope-aware scan of the eight copied-text bodies `RTr`, `Dcn`, `Gvr`, `Kvr`, `hTr`, `Zwr`, `nvr` and `vX` reports 82 unique external identifiers. This is an overlapping, first-level census, not 82 additional independent implementations or a transitive closure; the scratch scan input SHA-256 is `ba7a9a99d78e41d58a669d2adb00529d94c42c6da1c822f406cfbaa59f730eda`. These declarations and their transitive dependencies remain open before real policy integration.

## Shared read-block projection

A compiled composition probe supplied a `too-complex` ask to `O3e` with `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories:true` and an identity `sfn` fixture. With no declaration-prefix callback, `ior` returned the plain `other` ask because its copied `sfn` runs only inside the vector merge. Source `O3e` calls the same `sfn` immediately after every `D3e` result, including the initial result; the source body [3252890,3253132), SHA-256 `fd055389fe0fc6f6e0f3eb949bd38eb864ddb53322fc5237784e6564c40a7587`, and `VW` [4691,5003), SHA-256 `3d607c8b197353a1d5c1eb7d919aa664c78998d42ff3e111fe77a0c9e958b80f`, are already copied in the scheduler factory. The next generated change will expose that same closure from a shared factory so the vector wrapper and scheduler can use one per-instance combined `Cy && Tv` adapter; `createReadingScheduler` retains its existing signature. The composed fixture now observes the source `outsideReadsBlocked` hard ask through the vector and outer entry even in bypass mode when no vectors are enumerated, while the supplied exemption preserves the original ask object. Sixteen related tests, check/build and a compiled vector helper pass at identity `31c651834be2503812161cf6a7e57cba901d4d23dfb58155b7dc91b439613135`, inputs `21f41f0d71f5376261365732fa0ee8ac1e291679f7b806312d7dfbf7cddd65be`, validation `31de94e099365a719f53622f865af347e4cb1bbde29779b16b192e700a6d3b8b`, build `4bdae488cd942af8d57907f3ed6834b251227e4acd39fc5ee053cb92a5e729c1`. Plan SHA-256 `d51f55bee1eddca26cbe82e25b7e5d963e2a18c6d00285bfe1b9bebcf04d8cd0`, source `b7815f2a810446be9a8b170b83cb48cdeef0c44180c63f6c5cc90020e6f691dd`, ledger `69127be3b7f3152227efffcf98b4760f06ee5d83fcbccd151493aa455f524e79`, entry test `4d2d954f4ab31f5c72bf0077655236f067b0aae49b6e9757f9f08b689fcc2770`; the seven copied ranges are unchanged and the scope scan reports zero unresolved names. Exemption and all other policy dependencies remain caller-owned fixtures; independent review of this adapter change is pending.

## Vector scheduler unit

The next source unit is `ior` [3253708,3254351), together with `rfn` [3252331,3252731) and `sfn` [3252890,3253132), from `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` SHA-256 `4c74137eb23221981404d0ca87b765912f83344851d5c132b21857e5685b03da`. Their body SHA-256 values are `c6d9d9367c71cf0b36d2c97ba01aecc9feff97862e1586ccb65ae2d5df9ec11f`, `6b95a8942458ff1b1188c44c4aa197642c29578c7f88f6ffdd960bf58965139d`, and `fd055389fe0fc6f6e0f3eb949bd38eb864ddb53322fc5237784e6564c40a7587`. Source `VW` [4691,5003) is copied for read-block projection. The unit remains unconnected: an owned caller supplies the per-vector `O3e` and permission context, while a synchronous callback supplies the combined `Cy(input) && Tv()` sandbox exemption and a synchronous wrapper supplies the sampled-evaluation scope. The generated source sets `Tv=true`, so its `Cy` adapter must return that combined predicate, not `Cy` alone. These adapters preserve the call ordering; they do not claim the host has implemented `O3e`, sandbox policy, or permission-layer projection. The telemetry-only `wX`/`qun`/`ZF` sink is omitted from this local unit. Use separate owned callbacks per invocation so concurrent fixtures do not share mutable adapters. The data shape follows source outcomes: behavior plus optional decision reason, message, and rule; prefix vectors are arrays of booleans. The actual source tool's `checkPermissions` inside `ki`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [4337644,4338198), SHA-256 `d3123268383fc839696ea0248b2dad189f39d023f37f7c675e8eb8d508e7792e`, passes a result projection to `ior`; its `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` branch can replace an otherwise non-deny/non-ask result with a sandbox-override ask. The searched host Shell executor and permission files have no corresponding `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` or `shellType` input. ## Narrow integration review

A separate static reader traced the literal-prefix policy path through `ior`, `O3e`, `D3e` and `jcn`, conditional on successful parsing, exact rule matching, no clamp or read block, and nonblocking semantics/path/nested-shell checks. It confirmed the three-prefix all-allow result at the `ior` layer and the four-prefix soft-ask downgrade to passthrough at that same layer. It did not independently close `$pn` or the full helper graph, and its premise excluding semicolons does not cover the recorded three/four-command examples. An owned compiled analyser probe confirms single-command controls `A=1 B=2 C=3 export X` and `A=1 B=2 C=3 D=4 export X` have three/eight/exhaustive and four/six/incomplete readings respectively; the compiled host returns allow for each with an exact content allow. Every sampled vector remains simple and retains the literal command text. An owned compiled adapter that deliberately returns allow for each vector yields `ior` allow after nine calls for three prefixes and passthrough after seven calls for four. These inputs fit the reader's no-semicolon premise, but the adapter is a fixture, so its path, rule and final-entry assumptions still apply. The reader rejected a hand-written special case that produces those decisions without porting the source policy body. The separately recorded `$pn` trace supplies a conditional final ask for ordinary mode and no whole-tool allow; complete policy equivalence remains open.

## Independent source-port review

A read-only reviewer regenerated both plans from the extracted modules, verified all 146 analyser and ten merge body ranges and ledgers, checked the parser export and generator, and confirmed the source `Rl` other-reason branch matches the local merge adapter. The review found no source-copy mismatch; it did not run tests, build or the CLI. It identified missing durable assertions for the exact four-prefix vector order and merge message, which main added to the existing three test groups; the focused 11 tests and TypeScript check pass after that test-only change. The review also identified unresolved HOME and feature-state equivalence, non-ASCII source/runtime coordinate equivalence, and policy/child/hook integration. The Unicode test is an observation of the copied source with the product parser, not proof of original runtime equivalence. The reviewer made nine tool calls against the requested eight; this does not expand its reviewed scope.

An initial lexical range [56006,58198) included a following variable declaration; the exact range above is from `acorn`'s function node. `Dt` [21059,21250) and `E` [22531,34500) have body SHA-256 `1ad18968fe6537aa455606db77039fe0f8d6f6ea87130e9f76330d9ece1cb3a6` and `fbce9a1460bdf4f44df8413c5f77aa6509bfbc27204c08b98dc32e8bf97c5fc6`. Their full dependency closure has not been acquired. `E` and `Kt` can call `wd`, `chunk-j41p6s5m.js` [19620,19777), SHA-256 `35e25163f8efc08a9210f74751dd4c163911404d9583df40e48d061a8042f175`, which latches a subprocess environment-scrub setting. `E` can call `$Ts`, `chunk-0yczyn9c.js` [1957,2117), SHA-256 `eeebe37a9588fcbf624e7156bbdd2807d97f496ab414e32438b2120d05f435a6`, whose decision depends on a registered feature reader. `wd` calls `Ne`, `chunk-fdxhcr6b.js` [615,757), module SHA-256 `3c625e31827ca4ef7cc3a4691103fbbb3abbdd338778cbf70c0054f37359507b` matched to manifest, body SHA-256 `34ae1c66a8fdb94d6da2ddd1c82f382165d5bae7fff0df41d1e375f929032570`; this function accepts `1`, `true`, `yes` and `on` after case folding and trimming. The source latch holder is `Pe` [18909,19450), initialized as `T` [19450,19490); its reset clears the latched value. A focused `src` search found no matching subprocess-scrub setting; this does not establish the correct substitute for `wd`. The product mapping for `$Ts` is also unresolved.
- `Ze` [13062,17909), SHA-256 `3dcb11fb551708a094d1ae3da7fae669195e047301257b9e0f70a478824cec55`, is the declaration/unset precheck; `He` [11502,13002), SHA-256 `c1a4222c572fc8ca6b2c12eb9ad4be99d1e103d22abab226680adba9c107002e`, is another parse-tree precheck. `Ze` recursively checks both native declaration nodes and declarations represented as ordinary or wrapped commands, including operand and option cases. `He` recursively detects comment/parenthesis adjacency, case-pattern comments and function/keyword parentheses that can differ across shells. Their dependency behavior remains to be closed.
- `E6` snapshots `D`/`I`/`L`/`H`/`B`/`M`, initializes them for `Dt` and restores the prior values in `finally`. `E6`, `Dt`, `E`, `Kt` and `jt` are synchronous AST function declarations with no `await`; a port must preserve this ownership before asserting reentrant or concurrent behavior.
- `Zt` [47071,47094) and `Gt` [47094,47117) return `M` and `L`, body SHA-256s `65ebbd3655c51de87abdb1a3dc25693969c051c50e1f52b52206d8213182327e` and `c7e963308529ab6c53508d37411c0366f029cbb8174e8962bc3c40e43b71fb0b`. The inspected assignments occur in `jt` at 54936 (`M`) and 54745 (`L`); `F` [72396,72546), SHA-256 `2a6adb9071bd58b0d9939ecf1eb4ad8592be1e8540829a9ba699c0fbcfb375da`, sets the branch-conflict marker `B` at 72483.
- `ior`, chunk-sfn1dbxq.js [3253708,3254351), body SHA-256 `c6d9d9367c71cf0b36d2c97ba01aecc9feff97862e1586ccb65ae2d5df9ec11f`, invokes the analyser with its callback, passes collected values to `pyr`, evaluates the readings and merges their decisions. `O3e` [3254351,3255656), body SHA-256 `5e40fb332b89fb73b852edee56618b4533615bc35aa61f7d296be1fc666075d5`, calls `D3e` and then applies its permission-result wrappers. A resulting allow containing `&` is re-parsed by `ofn` [3252147,3252327), SHA-256 `a0e985439ce2989e12e411968ad0359ab9b37636115eb5a85a304365247e8440`, which distinguishes `binary_expression` from a background operator and treats an `ERROR` node as a hit. The source can then return a `backgroundOperator` safety ask; simply classifying every `&` identically would not preserve that branch. An owned compiled host decision probe with a literal allow for `echo hi &` observed allow for `echo hi &` in default and bypass modes, ask in ask mode, and deny in dontAsk; the unrelated `echo hi && echo bye` control returned allow in default mode; it did not execute either command or prove the source's full policy result. The compiled parser yields an `&` node for `echo hi &`, none for `&&`, and an `&` under `binary_expression` for arithmetic `1 & 2`; an invalid trailing `)` also yields an `ERROR` node. An owned copy of `ofn`, SHA-256 `1939197d98bd1666feef3ed73fa4f7a53ee6592963578a0fe761b460ba60548e`, returns true, false, false and true for those four trees respectively. The compiled analyser and copied semantics accept the background and `&&` control inputs as simple under the recorded defaults. These checks do not establish every surrounding `O3e` policy premise. `D3e` [3256051,3265763), body SHA-256 `ac3f93a8bc2c9233bb2feeff255ed38583d16b02152e43bd53c960436154e2ba`, supplies the optional prefix vector to `E6`. The main pass read its full body and selected dependencies for the candidate; independent readers checked the conditional merge and selected early returns, while the complete dependency graph and final-decision premises remain open. A naive AST census finds 78 distinct identifier call names in its body, including locals and nested calls; this is an upper-bound inventory, not a resolved dependency graph. This is not a single rule that rejects every declaration prefix.

## Rule recheck port in progress

The next isolated unit copies `$cn`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` SHA-256 `4c74137eb23221981404d0ca87b765912f83344851d5c132b21857e5685b03da`, UTF-16 [3253132,3253708), body SHA-256 `724ee7599692796f6c6d2c7aa85f60073db9bb760955e046a46c5ff1be9f1743`, and its nested-command walkers `Kg`/`ost`/`Emr`/`vmr` plus their literal constants from `chunk-m00pavz3.js` SHA-256 `e4dcdda42fdf15ff719179977326a414b62ad13d5992832859839c9203c16a62`. The exact copied `m0`/`utt` stay owned by the existing decision-merge module; its plan will expose `m0` without changing its body. A factory supplies the source-shaped `Gre` and `dA` rule-store functions per instance and the fixed source Bash tool identity. The source decision object and source rule objects remain intact, including nested `subcommandResults` and `matchedAskRule`; no host `PermissionDecision` projection or feature/sandbox default is inferred. Tests exercise the returned source function with owned rule-store adapters and the real copied matcher/parser, including deny priority, retained hard asks and nested candidates. This unit remains disconnected from ordinary authorization until the real store, context and upstream policy are closed. Independent review traced `$cn` and identified its existing copies and the missing store/projection boundaries; it did not rerun the earlier fixture.

The generated ten-range unit is `src/shell-rule-recheck-source.ts`, with plan SHA-256 `64b9fe6279bc84e2df5aadc0e5862f7dbace87765272ca4c8056675800aa6ec8`, source `96a0c56b1d436d1097c9aa61b8824343663614cbacc16ab237c579af76941ec5` and ledger `51b064282c3eb4be2c1d23f97bd0a6f0232f05d961ef29fe4a43eee80dd6e6f8`. The scope scanner reports zero unresolved identifiers. Exporting the already-copied `m0` changes only the merge plan's export line and generated module's export; their SHA-256 values are `7a6f873098572a24a0cee33264b9c410a7b890914a48822f52da1babb21bafa0` and `a450324c59e1397e755ec9546662d9c63dccaa7033b873899a89c68a6a36b355`, while the ten-range merge ledger stays `ef36be6fac679b472bbe92f9ac1059f4848d00690d1dcb61b54cf15a610cc532`. Two focused tests use the copied matcher with supplied rule maps and verify object identity, deny over a preceding ask, existing hard-ask retention, command and reason-map candidate order, and nested parsing at 16/17 levels and on either side of the accumulated character budget. Their test SHA-256 is `34dad53bc05709b2ddc7cccf8312bdfadf5bda4f891c530cec620836a4fde2dd`. Focused 2/2, check and build pass at identity `f175bdda7efb20c3c39c9839b068bda8ef324964267cf68d3d370efb97067774`, inputs `fe3987212605f36f93526486fead068122f4ca5fccb7bb4b305fe2abb3c8f865`, validation `ad084e29b21fb9184b37b60de72c26613945214373f15f7c8aa210c3102d04c4`, build `6fc111cfaa19321ee92ebfe681f8430e6fd0fb322c462a5cc197097c7c2c17bd`. The compiled ordinary-shell six-control driver and workflow-to-child clamp driver also pass at this identity; they exercise the existing connected paths, not the unconnected recheck. An earlier four-suite run passed 10/10 before the added character-budget assertions. The first type check rejected inline matcher options because the copied JavaScript's inferred signature omits optional properties; giving the same literal options named variables resolved the excess-property error without changing behavior. An independent reviewer returned `VERIFIED_SCOPED` after rehashing the manifest, replaying the ten recheck and ten merge ranges in memory, comparing both generated files and ledgers, scanning unresolved names, and rerunning the original two tests and check. The reviewer verified the injected call shapes, the exact `m0` binding and candidate order. They did not build, run the compiled CLI, or verify a production rule store. They identified missing multiple-ask, 10000/10001-character, redirect and `${ |...}` assertions. Main added these to the existing two tests: the first ask rule keeps its object identity, a later deny wins, the length boundary changes candidate enumeration, and both closed and unclosed nested forms plus a redirected compound produce the recorded candidates. The updated tests pass 2/2 and check at identity `9a03b67619f80781aae3aa2dbcdf2b1adfffb2dcacbef5269305448f5781b0c2`, inputs `a8e7db10fbe25c0f016a18917a1b6def1ee618f7188ceb1b3b6d07d3992ae255`, validation `b1f5b64cdef1c55356ba214583cf58ad3023ebe07acc5f2c73593a58209d3580`, build `3dcc48b9976964111e50e9a6c23bcde4fa001613b19bb4ae605e31ddc9e07326`, and test SHA-256 `174e256137fced0132b61b5c6478be1a5adf0d27fbad929e44819758e34f0500`. Main then instrumented the existing matcher adapter to assert that the full line, `echo ok`, and `printf later` each produce an ask candidate before the first rule is retained. The same two tests and check pass at identity `e7f9ce0c931685becdc9775df7474d90f95df3d1286d0e3ca20d97e5f921f546`, validation `3f3b7143aa7329e51d8f971297d6958000a5bc699eb48dd394b45780833ca6e3`, with unchanged product/build and test SHA-256 `424beeb3e69c66f6ff785bb936c351ca33c9baa20d979f48a079faf6b1e11301`. At the same identity the combined recheck, vector-policy and scheduler suites pass 8/8. The reviewer did not inspect those later assertions; the real rule-store and full policy remain open.

## Vector wrapper plan

After the `$cn` source-unit review begins, prepare `O3e`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3254351,3255656), body SHA-256 `5e40fb332b89fb73b852edee56618b4533615bc35aa61f7d296be1fc666075d5`, as a separate unconnected factory. It receives the still-unimplemented `D3e`, live effective-context reader `de`, source-shaped `sfn` and `$cn`, telemetry, message and clamp callbacks. It reuses copied `fTr`, and the `w6` parse function and its exact `Yx` sentinel must be exported together from the existing clamp module so their identity remains shared. `ofn` [3252147,3252327), `k9e` and `PHt` are copied from their recorded source ranges; the JSON string helper is the existing exact `_` export from `plugin-chain-store-carry-source.ts`, including its original no-op disposable. The wrapper preserves original ordering and result objects. Injected callbacks are a boundary for missing policy state, not a production implementation of `D3e`, sandbox or telemetry. This unit must remain outside ordinary authorization until those contracts are closed.

The four-range `src/shell-vector-policy-source.ts` has plan SHA-256 `75582d70c1178d58bba85410ae86cf7a9476c672601324cfbc56a0db3774c883`, source `0d86aa3cf7a462fe7151fa27ad1db66efa3d360ec21766e9a975f06bdc1432a4`, and ledger `93cb6ee64eaae1ab647ca3d2ad707c0b7b96e4010dc5160c91063c0b3c56dea2`; the scope scanner reports zero unresolved identifiers. The existing clamp plan and module now export `w6` and their shared `Yx`, with SHA-256 `464494e29ce4c4d832397dc89bbf26562b5f075c71c79c8959b49b6e6b581eb9` and `bf83223a5d99c5175dd1cd54c9d1df3fe71a3e9a23de6d7f03664242346f9b4c`; their eleven-range ledger is unchanged. Two focused tests cover clamp before `D3e`, unchanged argument forwarding, context reads, `sfn`/`$cn` order, sandbox-reason identity, `&&` and arithmetic controls, background operator, error node and overlength parser-abort paths. The test SHA-256 is `13ef2f10069cb566c0745cd351f259a8f28419cce6b8e7887800b852a766030f`. Nine related tests, check and build pass at identity `67fa43f903a106f3a833c141682b6f8b12cc5c9d9021f0ef5480ead180475922`, inputs `a8e7db10fbe25c0f016a18917a1b6def1ee618f7188ceb1b3b6d07d3992ae255`, validation `90d8946d8819ecfdfc0a8b69b9da0bed2c799cb5d84016c107562bd80171fcbf`, build `3dcc48b9976964111e50e9a6c23bcde4fa001613b19bb4ae605e31ddc9e07326`. The compiled ordinary-shell and workflow-to-child clamp drivers also pass at this identity through their existing connected paths. The tests inject policy and reason callbacks; they do not establish production policy equivalence. It visits initial/false/true vectors in order, preserves the deny-rule object for `echo $(rm target)`, and returns passthrough for `echo $(echo harmless)`. The three wrapper tests and check pass at identity `8d6607ad2bc019822e65551042589580381eb3af2cf49e126f4969951dc6c86d`, validation `fdf5a1727cec3094a66ebfda06340a6b3ef40ecd7c68e6a7d42a1d2f9aee065a`, with inputs and build unchanged. A further test confirms a `D3e` exception propagates as the same object without running the later projections; 3/3 and check pass at identity `69b02fbf643f541a4b735828c1705eb520d22fe36182e6ccf2ad6bcc2186aca1`, validation `fbecd4e11866014160689d8412bcdc3d944b2f1c52eaaa8c31b933e8c01352ae`, with unchanged inputs/build and test SHA-256 `b706ce4a7b947d0808c0eb4af428659ef7ba7f2e7c016c1dc720aabb70162940`. A final test asserts that an injected `sfn` projection replacing allow with ask is the value passed to `$cn`; the same 3/3 and check pass at `c4169a0af78e868af77ae02bbee97a696e9cd772160d8078374cacaef237abec`, validation `1d8dcb8d6f349ea8b4aa38ad2b4b2685c80760687e9453519300ce3a44055d38`, unchanged inputs/build and test SHA-256 `b459235429f7673b9e43c995dfdf19839f8de3a7310cfb9e0f52411327f89772`. The first plan-generation attempt looked for a nonexistent manifest digest field and stopped before writing; `decodedSha256` is the verified field. The reviewer did not inspect this later assertion. The callback and live-policy integration boundaries remain open.

## Rule and decision boundary

The next bounded host integration addresses the compiled wrapper-rule gap without replacing the existing permission chain. Source `dA` [3207029,3207522), SHA-256 `9109eaa354ba9e36b652456662b656fcc1a410db2ab444fa6efeb7da9b4bb3ef`, passes `stripAllEnvVars:true`, `skipCompoundCheck:true` and the deny/ask behavior to `xX`, [3205608,3207029), SHA-256 `33cf2d49f7c2fed00dd6b049837594d7b8c1ac1e8e98fef42d796aecbf7cc0d6`, in manifest-verified `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js`. The already reviewed matcher accepts an absent analysed command and then obtains the arguments through `SE`; this is its recovered fallback, not a new wrapper parser. The host adapter uses that fallback as an additional match for each existing shell deny or ask content rule; existing allow matching and all existing readings remain in place. Host `inputRules` retain their separate matching contract. The adapter applies to every existing Bash-family consumer through `contentRuleMatches`, while hook and classifier rechecks already call `permissionDecision` again. This is an incremental host projection, not a claim that source policy ordering, every shell construct, or the remaining path and sampling boundaries are closed. The new host regression failed before the change on `env CUSTOM=value printf denied > marker.txt` (`allow` rather than `deny`). The new compiled CLI cases also fail against the old build at that case, after the existing six cases and direct content denial passed. After the source matcher was bound, the five shell names pass direct, `env`, `command` and assignment deny/ask cases, lookup and unrelated-command controls, bypass denial and unchanged allow-rule controls. The compiled CLI passes its original six cases plus four content-deny cases, one ask and an unrelated wrapped-command control, using two owned loopback model requests per case. Related permission, child, skill and monitor suites pass 117/117; `npm run check` and `npm run build` pass. Identity `ad07eccfeb7e0b8a5e9bf991cf55d26eea69e1e7157e53fb66278719596204a8` binds inputs `631f97464d5e8412ba00a21c69d2d0b16368911026099317957d024d871ea064`, validation `5b0fb3ae2cc5985fc7f42a9b37e18a7c34e86689adc917decff454247b4a00b8` and build `d894c6394d10c06b574df57666b31d57d005223c8a2333b0fc05adecd12ee099`. Two later focused tests also pass after adding both `PermissionRequest` and `PreToolUse` hook rewrites in a real child registry, plus a skill preflight that must block both the wrapped command and an earlier marker while allowing an unrelated wrapped command. Check passes at identity `5b846efab7b5881034c4609bf66e3619e9bc5aade9774b86a04fc67bbbdc1e34`, validation `9aee41cde1ea1dc314c71d90233c964c10168ee6ffd9e1ae60df1cab75c9bab2`, with unchanged product and build. A bounded independent review returned `VERIFIED_SCOPED` for the deny/ask dA/xX call shape and wrapper candidates; it did not run the CLI or review the later sampled-denial adapter. The reader observed source-consistent overmatching for `env -v printf` and `sudo -l printf`, and a matcher-level miss for `bash -c 'printf denied'`. A later owned host `permissionDecision` probe confirms allow in default and bypass for `bash -c`, `sh -c` and `command bash -c` with `Bash(printf:*)` deny; ask and dontAsk modes apply their general hold. No nested command was executed. A later owned fixture using the copied `Nwe`/`nfn`/`Fcn` and an injected deny callback finds that both printf scripts return null without calling the nested policy, while `sh -c 'rm marker'` invokes it and returns a `dangerousRemoval` ask. The compiled analyser marks both direct scripts simple, and the copied semantics accepts them with `sourceGlobRecord:true`. An owned source-store probe with explicit source-shaped `Bash(printf:*)` deny also reports no exact or analysed-subcommand rule match for either printf wrapper, while direct `printf denied` matches the subcommand deny. `RTr` retains the analyser's full command text in this case rather than extracting the `-c` body. The same owned recursion fixture returns a `dangerousRemoval` ask for `command sh -c 'rm marker'` and `env X=1 sh -c 'rm marker'`; the latter nested callback receives `export X=1 && rm marker`. A separate host decision probe with `Bash(rm:*)` deny returns allow for all three rm forms in default and bypass. No shell command was executed. These probes do not establish the complete source `D3e`/`$pn` result; they show why the source dangerous-removal recursion alone is not a general nested-deny adapter. A possible parser-exception boundary in later host reading checks remains unconfirmed. The host retains deny-first priority where the fixture-only source `jcn` alone checks exact ask first; a complete source decision must be traced through `D3e` and its wrapper before claiming equivalence.

The source Bash method `checkPermissions`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [4337644,4338198), body SHA-256 `d3123268383fc839696ea0248b2dad189f39d023f37f7c675e8eb8d508e7792e`, stashes a recognized `sed -i` substitution target through `KMe` [4319525,4321079), body SHA-256 `579573fab3b51702646b3bafde55c9a5ec95ea72ebde530194e90b90e6ffcd2e`, before calling `ior`, then supplies a per-decision projection for `dangerouslyDisableSandbox`. That projection leaves deny/ask and rule-only allows intact, and may turn another outcome into a `sandboxOverride` ask. The local `run_command` schema instead accepts `command` and `timeout_ms`, and executes `/bin/sh` without that source sandbox field; preserving the product's existing input contract and write-history ownership requires an explicit local boundary rather than feeding arbitrary source options into the host.

The outer entry's input boundary is separate from vector checking. Source `$pn` [3360771,3364305) calls `Zwe` [3355651,3356264), body SHA-256 `95e29a86fccb34d21a6068f2a631fba82b957a75beca62b96f3bfe365873fc1a`, before the tool permission method. `Zwe` parses with the tool schema and may retry after setting aside unrecognized keys when the source feature and call predicates allow it; `$pn` marks a non-deny result incomplete when keys were set aside. The host currently passes the incoming input to `permissionDecision`; source input-schema compatibility and the effect of an incomplete result on each local entrypoint require their own mapping before full integration.

`KMe` also calls `THn`, `chunk-m00pavz3.js` [8291,8842), SHA-256 `d0d13983c2fcbca6be571b4294b84f62214c57fa8f39937d931104b43020a96b`, which rejects overlength, parser-error, expansion, complex redirect and untrusted argument forms before `KMe` classifies a single `sed -i` substitution. `THn` calls source `R8t`/`CHn`, `L`, `Nqe`, `S`, `N`, `A`, `b`, `nde` and `w`; its copied regex dependencies in the analyser and `nde` in the matcher can be shared by explicit exports. The dedicated unconnected source-copy unit now retains 19 source ranges and reuses the analyser's nine copied regexes and matcher's `nde`/`y`/`SE` through unchanged-body exports. Its platform and network-path predicates remain explicit callbacks, so the Windows control uses a supplied fixture rather than claiming the original `PA` implementation. Plan SHA-256 `7f3788d1186c1859cc2df4ca8e4650a2917671389ba11829de9f72e0ec5d3124`, source `e90bdac3e70d1ca56c65c21a7208808a01e78860984c8b7c636749f90b11363c`, ledger `700abbbf80cabb7ac30a6cbca89afbb02bb3360222ce121474baccb848b769af`, test `1495495b8c152b8cd43681acfbfb787a17863a5e5c8b11bc0d684ec9eb17c1b4`. The analyser plan/source hashes are `ff163b102c8f198e139aa9cc3bf7b29467ff30d1ab31434418e9969795346053`/`ad85851ce3580aa33d946e286779235b5d6c294eebc22b6e41fd99f0a7133ee4`; matcher plan/source `37839bca0951dbdaf31cd72b4832d2eaff5a7a3feb85fd392e464ea9e482d65c`/`9396debbf70210d473f26b20b5ee2ed14b049102a1691f800a02458ef4c4d6da`. Tests do not execute the identified commands. The source shell `call` consumes the stash with the same tool-use ID and resolved `KMe` path at `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` UTF-16 offset 4338373 before shell execution. The shared file-tool consumer `mY`, `chunk-f606a53w.js` offset 4715, consumes approved path spellings, recognizes an expired sentinel, and otherwise obtains fresh spellings. Root sessions own a new stash while forks inherit the root stash (`chunk-4bw62nzm.js` offset 55743, module SHA-256 `1a8788672a623975dc4a807604528e46b128f16b5ec159495c0ec2370f805713`; `chunk-f606a53w.js` SHA-256 `dd0a1dc61be769e7ea2ec7f4345b3b4f88996150d4d219b8ada90f03e89dd033`); both module hashes match the extraction manifest. The complete source container `Be` is [51037,52888), SHA-256 `94cc56f72b64bd393d6de38c2b8dffb1eec6ace8b5cad81ea3585c851d880c63`; its key helper `He` is [52888,52926), `e1cb0560eb8206bbd1d129baeaff3e5b6606b2cc52e2b01f60c52947d98df7a6`, and its expired sentinel `j8e` is [50994,51037), `93a08f28d517469686fc89f07d3185d6c7386aff669137d1562c66499a49648d`. The complete consumer `mY` is `chunk-f606a53w.js` [4672,5095), SHA-256 `386d67a9a20b8a254c72fcf7d0335042bfca32ccda41aff37f9c1d23c45244ef`. `Be` keys paths by tool-use ID plus NUL plus resolved path, keeps independent read/write lanes, intersects repeated approvals, evicts the oldest at 256 entries by default and remembers evicted keys up to 1048576 before poisoning the lane. Its read identity map separately records device/inode disagreements as null; consuming a read clears the call's identities, and consuming either lane clears that call's entries. `mY` throws on the expired sentinel and resolves fresh spellings when no approval record exists. The Shell call invokes `consume` for its recognized sed target without checking the return, while file-tool consumers use `mY`; these observations do not establish complete filesystem-race behavior. These are path-approval records, not file-content snapshots. The host's `Session.touchFile` records file-history metadata after writes, and `automaticCheckpoint` runs at a turn boundary; neither establishes this per-tool path owner. This recognizer therefore remains unconnected, and stash lifecycle, expiry, identity and host mapping need a dedicated source-backed chain. Independent review and production consumer mapping remain open.

A focused compiled helper probe also exposes a priority boundary. With `Bash(printf:*)` deny and an exact ask for each whole command, the copied `jcn` returns the exact ask object for `printf ok`, `command printf ok`, and `env CUSTOM=value printf ok` because `Uwe` returns an exact ask before `jcn` tests prefix rules. The fixture uses default mode, source-shaped local rule maps and throwing callbacks for later path/safety checks; it does not establish `D3e`'s full result or execute a command. Consequently, attaching the copied wrapper matcher to the host's existing deny-first branch would change this source-backed early-return order.

An owned 11-range rule-store fixture combines the copied `Gre`/`zM`/`B_e`/`uNe`/`U0`/`dA`/`Uwe` bodies with the 49-range `xX` fixture and the existing compiled rule parser. Its policy uses default mode, a non-MCP Bash tool, no evaluation confinement or protected-tool filtering, and fixture message/suggestion callbacks; it does not establish those excluded branches or run a shell. With `Bash(rm:*)` first in user settings, `Bash(r*)` next, and a duplicate `Bash(rm:*)` in project settings, `Gre` keeps key order `rm:*`, `r*` while the first entry carries the project source. For `rm target` and the three recorded `timeout`, `command` and `env` wrappers, the exact deny search does not match these prefix rules, while the source `dA` prefix search returns both with the project rule first. With matching exact rules across behaviors, `Uwe` chooses deny before ask before allow, then passthrough when no rule matches. The non-MCP `Gp`, mode and protected-tool predicates are fixture adapters; `jcn`, path checks, outer mode handling and the final verdict are outside this probe. Plan SHA-256 `4ce3e6dae8236797928adbf3d8986e41d44df2294214514ec13420a369cb5c54`, generated fixture `0d4db3871ec1b4ad2e12b077019da91794fd1942403ec21935701815f4267930`, ledger `33db8aae22edab8f6a9d529043a994d3c578fc672d8224123b4008cf8c3fea5b`; the scope-aware scan reports zero unresolved identifiers. The compiled workflow-to-child baseline exposed a host data-shape defect before the child started. `workflow-script` normalized a flat `bashCommandClamp` list into one group, then `AgentRegistry.launch` rejected that nested list when it revalidated the external option. Source `chunk-hedqf2m1.js` SHA-256 `01fea1c4f8ed9442ca18c30704c8533a1f037b800cc4faae2b4ecaaa273f5a56` validates, deduplicates and sorts the flat list at UTF-16 [46284,47451), then adds it as a single permission layer at [62642,62783). The host workflow bridge now forwards that flat validated list and the child registry still performs its own validation and groups it. The targeted workflow option test and TypeScript check passed. After rebuilding, the owned compiled workflow reached the child command and reproduced the expected old-gate mismatch: `command echo clamped-ok; printf second` was denied as one unmatched line despite source `fTr` matching both segments. The compiled driver and the PermissionRequest rewrite regression provided pre-integration baselines; the source-copy review and integrated driver subsequently passed as recorded below. An owned compiled helper probe with `HOME` absent found `fTr` accepts `$HOME/bin/echo escaped` if its group contains both that literal text and `${os.homedir()}/bin/echo escaped`, while `/bin/sh` expands the absent variable to empty and would resolve `/bin/echo`. `E6` `Z` [68158,68694) assumes `os.homedir()` for HOME even when the shell environment has no HOME. With a whitespace-containing HOME, the unquoted expansion is instead unverifiable. This is a source/host execution discrepancy: a source-matched command can name a different executable. The integrated boundary preserves the legacy `shellReadings`/`clampAdmits` decision whenever HOME is absent, then applies copied `fTr`; for a present HOME, it pins that exact value in the individual tool call so a later environment change cannot invalidate the check. This is a host adaptation to preserve the existing restriction, not a claim that the source has that guard. Both the original and PermissionRequest-updated inputs get a check. The chosen value reaches only the executing tool context and is not written to the journal; unrelated environment fields retain their existing behavior. This probe executes no supplied shell command.

At the source outer boundary, `sor` [3208689,3208983) returns a deny if a shell permission check throws while any `bashCommandClamps` remain, and the shell definition calls it from `permissionCheckFailureDecision` [4337559,4337643). `EJo` [3208297,3208689) denies execution surfaces that cannot match the clamp. The host child pool currently removes `run_background` and `monitor` through `PARENT_CONTROLS`; its `authorize` function already checks a non-string command as unverifiable. Any child-gate migration must preserve those boundaries and keep an unexpected source-check exception from reaching execution. Source `O3e` performs `fTr` before `D3e`, with the same JSON-stringified failed span currently used by `bashCommandClampDenial`; it reads the current context's clamp groups on each call. The source workflow child adds a `{kind:"bash_command_clamp",rules:be}` permission layer after existing layers (`chunk-hedqf2m1.js` UTF-16 [62642,62783), declarator), and `de`, `chunk-p46ep603.js` [4120,5234), appends each such layer to `bashCommandClamps` in order. The host's `childConfig` clones inherited configuration, but `AgentRegistry.launch` replaces `bashCommandClamp` with the newly supplied group. A nested constrained spawn is therefore an open host/source propagation boundary if the caller supplies a new group; the actual active child tool pool and exposure need a dedicated reachable-chain test before changing it. The ordinary child tool pool also replaces the model-facing skill execution callback through `createSkillTool(...).forChild()` with the instruction-only skill tool; `expandedBody` and its shell runner are therefore not reached through that ordinary child tool. A focused child-tool test passes with an inline `touch` directive returned as text, zero parent callback calls and no marker file; check passes at identity `1394b93d43197813b87bf8068520a12224af42e640aa44df0e29d8faf259ead4`, validation `7cf253f5bf59ead40eaec3f98e58aa5053165c45a88203640bd5eb1a3004502e`, with unchanged product and build. Other skill entrypoints remain part of the ordinary-policy migration. The source layer declarator has body SHA-256 `5bcf5b0a0574d851dcb734236287c84dda48467cde8ffeba11a1d04cffc29b2a` and module SHA-256 `01fea1c4f8ed9442ca18c30704c8533a1f037b800cc4faae2b4ecaaa273f5a56`; `de` has body `b36b198420ff43a4f53ccbb2a55e75946883b414595bba38684a341c59b52b88` and module `60e85bbf84620f367d2dc51d5d1c9951444bc61f1fef9c34fc6b9b43058edb36`. `sor`, `EJo` and the shell failure method have body SHA-256s `1168a36d4e919fbb31fabfd65ef88164b5609929bc12d7b04600b56cd4915649`, `bafab385e9a56624a25fd209e73bd7e7717b70fb816fc761ef1e51a2ad1fd29a` and `fe51f4f8b2c1fb746ad21d0c349867a35e7a5325178196115de58a58a94cbd94`.

An owned 28-range pipeline fixture adds copied `Gvr`, `qvr`, safety-priority and decision-identity helpers to the compound-structure fixture. For `echo 雪 | cat > /fixture/out`, its callback sees `echo 雪` and the redirect-stripped `cat` in order; two allow results combine to allow, a segment deny wins, and a passthrough segment yields ask. The fixture sets the `CX` feature false, supplies the callback outcomes and omits cd/git hazards; its parser fallback uses the compiled parser. It therefore checks the merge under those conditions, not the full per-segment `O3e` or filesystem policy. Plan SHA-256 `c3e4e47a898e1823c33298a9705d25e79cda7b275217b162ca52dad14e493baf`, fixture `a5c7c7a204ceead0ec2205194127ff07acf08a7d59b547a83110cb1978d21884`, ledger `b026749c96750ffaa6a590b55c38fb9a982e5eb88df4d56a14f49438c48c423e`; the scope scan reports zero unresolved names.

An owned 17-range compound-structure fixture copies `Rcn`/`Kvr`, `z5e`/`Eun`, quote and tree visitors, and uses the compiled parser. With a supplied tree, `echo ok; printf hi` returns no-pipeline passthrough, while a subshell and a brace group return `shell-operators` ask. A Unicode pipeline `echo 雪 | cat > /fixture/out` preserves the two segments and removes the output redirect using the source UTF-8 byte slicing. The pipeline-policy, fallback parser and message formatting are fixture adapters; no pipeline permission verdict or all quote-coordinate behavior is established. The owned inline-recursion fixture used an incorrect `i3e=8` adapter: the original declarator is `i3e=2`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3117206,3117211), SHA-256 `aea9e6f48ed32e8add30735fdcef21718198797aa165a76f7e62fd98859f02a4`. A separate 33-range owned inline-recursion fixture copies `Nwe`/`nfn`/`Fcn`, source parsing and the recognition bodies. An owned 18-range inline-shell recognition fixture copies `FTr`, `Jcn`, `Dwe`, `Lwe` and their source lexical dependencies, with Node `os.homedir`, a no-op telemetry callback and the `F` count adapter. `sh -c "rm target"` produces a judged script, while `sh -c "alias z=rm; z target"` produces an unjudged script; both satisfy the copied dangerous-removal predicate. A harmless echo script does not, and a Python command is not a recognized shell. Both `Lwe` variants return a non-classifier-approvable `dangerousRemoval` ask. The compiled analyser yields simple argv arrays for the two shell literals. This does not run those commands or the complete `Fcn`/`nfn` recursion and budget path; telemetry was suppressed in the fixture. A direct host decision probe with `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories:true`, workspace `/fixture/work`, no rules, and `cat /fixture/outside/input` returns allow in default and bypass modes; dontAsk denies through its general approval gate. The owned source `nvr`/path-fence fixture returns passthrough for `/fixture/work/input` and an `outsideReadsBlocked` safety ask for `/fixture/outside/input`. The compiled `parseSettings` retains this flag in a permissions object. The same decisions are reproduced by the compiled `permissionDecision` helper at identity `b6e0ea24`. This compares a host decision with a source-layer fixture; the full source `D3e` verdict and compiled CLI path remain unverified. Host `Session.workingDirectories(true)` filters project-added roots for file tools, but `permissionDecision` does not read the flag for Shell; the source path check also needs identity spellings and `Xce` exceptions before integration. In the same explicit fixture, a project-settings-added directory asks, a session-added directory passes, a fixture exception passes, and a fixture Read deny returns deny before the fence. These are path-layer observations with synthetic callbacks, not final source or CLI outcomes.

An owned 13-range `GTr`/`Vcn` rule-recheck fixture uses the same rule-store adapters and the compiled analyser. For `echo ok; find . -rm`, `gyr` refuses under the null-reader default; `GTr` returns a subcommand `Bash(find:*)` deny even alongside an exact full-line allow. With no deny, it returns that exact allow, returns null for a prefix-only `Bash(echo:*)` allow, and returns the session ask for `Bash(echo:*)`. `D3e` may still apply read-block, nested-shell and other checks after a non-deny `GTr` result, so these are helper outcomes rather than final permissions. `D3e` invokes the declaration-prefix callback before its `too-complex` return and before rule, semantics and path decisions. An owned compiled composition probe (`w385-four-prefix-policy-probe.mjs`, SHA-256 `b356d0ebf769b549799d2b272503c08be6f4dfee8fc6a8b8a3d19d784e647cc2`) combines the copied `E6`, `ior` and `$pn` with supplied allow decisions for every vector. The host fixture uses ordinary `Bash(full line)` content rules without a whole-tool allow and was configured in default mode. The earlier subcommand-denial citation [3262269,3262604) cut through the returned expression; [3262265,3262627) above now records the complete statement and digest. ## Sampled-denial integration plan

Environment binding follow-up: an owned skill invocation preflights `X=old; X=new export Y; printf "$HOME" > marker.txt` with `HOME=/allowed-fixture` and a deny for `printf /denied-fixture`. Approval of a later `true` changes HOME; the first command then writes `/denied-fixture` despite the deny. No recovered entrypoint was executed. Source `iye`, chunk-sfn1dbxq.js [3368429,3370821), SHA-256 `b7e7d086e93c72e32742a77033691e893b73479b7aa477dc794b6ff0759ecd56`, checks each span via `Bu` and then calls the shell tool; source `Z`, chunk-zjewzgfh.js [68158,68694), SHA-256 `a3e16a9b07f2129860dc33a00ecc4b95faf0096225c97876bd41dd0fb18f6ab9`, reads HOME from the host at analysis time. The host executes spans sequentially after preflighting all of them, an existing W06 adaptation. Bind the HOME value observed for each approval to that command's owned child environment so later callbacks and spans cannot change the value on which its sampled denial relied. Keep the source tool-call boundary and host preflight, allow a fresh value for each span, and preserve missing HOME as missing. Verify by rerunning the skill fixture, including an unaffected control and sequential approval values. An owned `Agent` probe then changed HOME from `/allowed-fixture` to `/denied-fixture` in the `tool: <name>` status callback after permission checking. `run_command`, `run_background` and command `monitor` each executed the latter value under a `Bash(printf /denied-fixture)` denial. Bind the HOME observed by each successful authorization to its execution context, replacing it if a hook changes the command and it is reauthorized. The existing foreground clamp already passes `shellHome`; extend that handoff to ordinary shell commands and consume the same value in the background registry. Preserve WebSocket monitor behavior and allow a later tool call to obtain a fresh value. Source Bash `checkPermissions` [4337644,4338198) invokes `ior`, and its `call` follows at [4338198,4347621), in the manifest-verified `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js`; this host binding is an explicit adaptation to keep the value used by `Z` with the value spawned, not a claim of source-wide environment parity.

The next bounded integration carries only deny decisions from the source declaration vectors into the existing ordinary Shell gate. `E6`, `chunk-zjewzgfh.js` [7897,10857), SHA-256 `6d959cd10dfbfb7199bf45f47b93da07a657f6b7db767a10936c06ea2fed85a8`, captures prefix count even on a too-complex initial result; `pyr` [7547,7897), `f617f6b4c7bfc467796b1bd33b87f6c436842d505299fcceb3f30ec6f5fb2bfa`, supplies source vectors. `D3e` [3256051,3265763) calls its subcommand policy on the resulting command objects, `dA` [3207029,3207522) checks deny rules through `xX`, and `DTr` [3234534,3234967) lets any sampled deny win. The host already has policy layers and its separate `inputRules`; the adapter checks each successfully analysed sampled command using the existing denial path, without treating a sampled allow or ask as evidence for a broader source decision. Source rule-bucket ordering and the host's boolean policy projection are distinct as described below. It retains the existing baseline and allow behavior in all other cases. This adapts the source denial projection to the host's synchronous `permissionDecision`; it does not replace `ior`, change the shared clamp completeness flag, or claim complete policy precedence. Parser, HOME and failed-analysis boundaries remain independently visible. The fixture `X=old; X=new export Y; printf "$X" > marker.txt` produces `printf old` and `printf new`, and local `/bin/sh` prints `new`; the current bypass host allows it despite `Bash(printf new)` denial. The initial host regression failed with `ask` rather than deny in default mode; the compiled bypass case ran on the pre-sampling build. The adapter now denies the `printf new` vector in default, bypass, ask and dontAsk modes, honors a host input-rule denial and handles the recorded Unicode case whose other vector is too complex. A bypass no-match control retains allow. The compiled CLI now blocks the sampled denial and executes the unrelated-rule control, whose actual `/bin/sh` output is `new`; all prior wrapper and reading controls pass. The related nine suites pass 130/130, check and build pass at identity `2e0d3ee482cf0a919fd12e591ee5582d433ce9765202a32fbb7c9bf47accfa06`, inputs `468870762938ad0b5b3332a143fb9054853ea33b59bdd8d204edc98c6e2daaa4`, validation `5b76a64a2dbbb8b867067f62e5726e353968e6055e57a5e50dda91360be0ae1f`, build `3cbcd7dcfb4e63a153c9ea89ce2c31967eda5df391a969cbe91476d2856be419`. A later environment probe showed that with HOME absent the copied analyser substitutes Node's account-directory fallback for `$HOME`, while the local `/bin/sh` sees an empty value; an owned host regression reproduced a denial on that wrong candidate. The updated adapter skips dynamic-argv sampled candidates when HOME is absent and marks that sample uncertain; too-complex vectors and sampling exceptions do the same. This also holds dynamic-argv candidates that do not use HOME when HOME is absent, an explicit conservative scope. It still evaluates existing deny readings first, then holds an otherwise allowed command for approval even in bypass. This is a conservative host adaptation, not a claim about the source failure posture; it does not establish a stable HOME value through later hooks or execution. A four-prefix no-match case retains the old default allow, preserving the separately open incomplete-sampling behavior. The focused environment/parser and source regression set passed 38/38 with check at this stage. The later independent integration review and repair are recorded below.

Compiled read-block observation: an owned loopback CLI driver with `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories:true` ran `cat` against a synthetic file outside the workspace and wrote its contents in both default and bypass modes; an inside-workspace control also ran. The driver has SHA-256 `6e52b04325a3361a747f19c13a9cfaf659cade81d64f26c1a5cf9e08e970d0db` and used compiled build identity `777020ee`; it cleaned up its synthetic files. This confirms the current host behavior, while the full source path verdict and exception mapping remain open.

The environment handoff now covers ordinary foreground, background and command-monitor execution, the existing child clamp, and each preflighted skill span. A changed HOME during auto classification triggers a new authorization rather than carrying the prior verdict into a different environment. The owned status-callback fixture covers all three tool runners, the auto-classifier fixture observes a changed HOME being denied before the marker, and the skill fixture covers a later approval mutation, a later command rechecked at the new value and an absent HOME. The first foreground regression failed before the handoff. `npm run check`, `npm run build`, and nine related test files pass 182/182. Compiled `smoke:shell-readings-cli -- --compiled` and `smoke:skill-inline-cli -- --compiled` pass through owned loopback gateways; those CLI drivers do not exercise the environment mutation. Identity `777020ee7bfcf1b53dee8eed1f5840e9469ef2a7c7988eef8f988258a212d16b` binds inputs `7f47aa279ea7c687932eccdaf674a258967ec44db15eadb1ccd559ef3def6d7e`, validation `ffb4c34d6dcd8d75713bbd02169d4d89737c823ddeba98d6de22fcae0226e025`, and build `b92dd18d14786a3b222f1cae49d30b10f06c3643206e798dad0c32dffb196e2d`. A subsequent independent review checked the sampled-denial and environment adapters and found the missing-HOME assignment issue recorded below. Full source scheduling, incomplete-vector handling and other permission branches remain open.

Independent sampled-denial review at identity `777020ee` verified the exercised parser, HOME and handoff paths, but found a missing-HOME assignment case outside the argv guard. `Kt` [56006,58185), SHA-256 `49d296837cdb1174371ead43461bb39e9917368b2c6d5185ef25bebc63a6c6a8`, builds sampled text from `envVars`; `xe` [65986,67564), SHA-256 `52110a65d4e7c628424063ddfa03b9683489d21758f084b15fb2008a44ad9be4`, expands an assignment without incrementing the argv expansion counter, so `argvSourceLiteral` can remain true while its text contains the Node HOME fallback. With HOME absent, `X=$HOME export Y` is consequently checked as `X=<account directory> export Y`, though the local shell sees an empty value. The reviewer reproduced both a fallback-path false denial and an allow against the empty-assignment rule. Conservatively skip sampled candidates when HOME is absent and the complete command contains `$`, then retain the existing uncertainty hold after checking original deny readings. This host guard also holds literal dollar signs and unrelated expansions; the complete source policy is still open. A new host regression failed on the fallback-path false deny before the guard; after the fix, fallback, empty-assignment and unrelated rules all hold for approval, and a direct explicit denial still wins. Check/build, 182 related tests and compiled Shell and skill CLI drivers pass at identity `834e10bb21d67011132a5d73723d450c3f9a43b82dcc8964fb90802e22080add`, inputs `7309679d91f89016628494d7837af0c8ac1e9cf2531164b9ddaf21ebbbb9d438`, validation `5a1e60b91fbdd8758ddb1a88622ec10d7282b2df18df066b101d8bd1aa6a60a7`, build `b7a71f822740fec92c5ec250104aa6803280da3594c851351858c0f327cb12a2`. These CLI drivers do not exercise the missing-HOME assignment. The reviewer did not run build or compiled CLI; its follow-up recheck ended early because the agent's API budget was exhausted, so that repair has no independent recheck. A main-thread decision probe independently exercised the assignment and two later-argument forms with HOME absent; all three retained the readings ask for fallback, empty-assignment and unrelated deny rules. Its four-prefix non-exhaustive example remains an open declared sampling boundary. A later owned skill invocation confirmed its skill-hook environment concern: preflight approves a `SessionStart` hook while HOME is `/allowed-fixture`, a subsequent hook approval changes HOME to `/denied-fixture`, and the first hook writes the latter despite a sampled `Bash(printf /denied-fixture)` deny. This is distinct from the skill body span fix. Hooks use `src/hooks.ts`'s `spawn` with live `process.env`, and async hooks share or adopt that runner. Source skill registration `qe`, `chunk-htgmc2p1.js` SHA-256 `fa11fb18ab034759b746d2f6ae456b604470a7c017bc53d3a03c25f799207ba3`, [3899,4257), body SHA-256 `d8887c5d75042a1a8ab942747fd29fcd9afe91ddaf62b6f174911e1c8ea01def`, associates each source hook definition with session registration and its skill root. The local flat/group hook identity additionally depends on event, original position and definition digest; configured async hooks are cloned and re-enter `runHooks`, while header-adopted hooks reuse an already spawned handle. Any local repair needs to retain the approved HOME by definition across both paths without serializing it into the session or applying it to unrelated parent hooks. The source hook execution and registration lifetime are not yet fully mapped. An owned background-skill/AgentRegistry probe used an ask rule whose approval callback changed HOME after the wrapper's permission check. The spawned command wrote `/allowed-fixture`, matching the outer Agent authorization's pinned execution context rather than the callback's `/denied-fixture`; the child completed with two model calls. This covers that one registry path, not all wrappers or inherited policy changes.

An independent follow-up of the absent-HOME adapter returned `NOT_VERIFIED`. It reported that the command-wide `$` guard skips a deterministically sampled `printf new` denial and that the uncertainty hold also applies under bypass with an unrelated content rule. Main reproduced both decision results with HOME absent. The hold was an explicit conservative host adaptation, so an unrelated-variable case can be repaired without treating every incomplete source sample as bypass-safe. The review also identified a clamp recheck that fails when HOME changes after approval and before the final check; that path still needs a runtime reproduction. The reviewer ran four focused tests and a Node environment probe, but did not drive these findings through the CLI. Source `Z` [68158,68694), body SHA-256 `a3e16a9b07f2129860dc33a00ecc4b95faf0096225c97876bd41dd0fb18f6ab9`, calls `ht()` only when an untracked `HOME` is expanded. Main verified the module digest and compared unchanged copied `E6`/`pyr` vectors with HOME absent and empty: `$X` vectors were identical, while `$HOME` yielded the account-directory fallback versus the empty value seen by the local shell. The proposed host adapter binds a per-call HOME value at the `ht` import boundary and keeps the recovered declarations unchanged; the scope must remain isolated across concurrent callers. A separate approval-to-execution snapshot supplies the same value to any final clamp recheck. These observations do not close vector completeness, source path policy or all environment-sensitive Shell behavior.

Unscoped source-unit callers retain `os.homedir()`, while ordinary Shell sampling supplies the current execution HOME and maps an absent value to the empty value observed by local `/bin/sh`. A child clamp uses its approved HOME in the same scope across its asynchronous source call, retaining the existing legacy guard when HOME is absent. At this earlier identity the typed adapter SHA-256 was `da93ab753b4747e3363a566e538eecc612e0493e626c3c919b5acd93cf661267`; the later alternate-scope change has a different digest recorded below. Related analyser, permission and clamp tests pass 17/17; Agent, restriction, skill, hook, async-hook, registry and subagent suites pass 247/247; check/build and both compiled Shell/skill drivers pass at identity `5a56d4a30fc074200437be4908ca43df18a50dcb191c5437c703fa97e82ffe3f` (inputs `9c31b2802d894dbec02cf3413b4b3957aeea170be1f150b00a2e8592c1472ba6`, validation `22885a4b2b14b6e5babcf605dedd1894b21de20bf6227fc49ae43fe4b98e25a8`, build `f08c93ff3d893eff3e9afa429ed369905bea5344158d921288d91d32f23c4beb`).
