Custom skill-shell follow-up: on this macOS host with HOME absent, `/bin/sh` and `/bin/bash` expose an empty `$HOME`, while `/bin/zsh` initializes it to the account directory; an explicitly empty HOME remains empty in all three observations. A real `executeSkill` regression with `shell:'/bin/zsh'` confirmed that the ordinary empty-HOME projection allows a sampled account-directory deny and the skill runs. Source `iye`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3368429,3370821), SHA-256 `b7e7d086e93c72e32742a77033691e893b73479b7aa477dc794b6ff0759ecd56`, checks a selected Bash or PowerShell tool and then calls that same tool; the host instead supports an arbitrary executable in `SkillInfo.shell`, so its custom-shell admission is an explicit host adaptation. Source `Z` delegates HOME expansion to `ht` as recorded above. For a non-default skill executable with HOME absent, retain the source account-directory projection and check the host empty-HOME projection too; any deny wins, an ask is retained, and only two allows pass. The process still receives the exact approved environment, so explicit empty HOME stays distinct from an absent value. This is a conservative two-observation host adapter, not a claim that every arbitrary shell has only these HOME behaviors. Skill hooks run through the host's fixed `/bin/sh` and retain the ordinary projection. The first actual skill regression failed before this adaptation, and its marker was cleaned by the owned fixture.

The custom-shell adaptation now passes an actual `executeSkill` fixture covering missing-HOME zsh denial, missing-HOME sh execution, missing-HOME bash denial and explicitly empty zsh HOME. The updated compiled skill CLI driver installs an owned local plugin, invokes its bare skill name with HOME absent, and verifies denial, an absent marker and zero new model requests. The first driver used a qualified name while this default catalog publishes the bare name; that false run reached the model and is excluded from the red baseline. The runtime fixture above is the reproduced baseline. The final related suites pass 265/265, check/build pass, and compiled Shell and skill CLI drivers pass at `43de69b67c3a3a9ae894d5d232c8fd6cc3a8927a3708487e71910913d8314e89` (inputs `245bc46a3e53ef61616c99932692a73b21f6054f35d16d33d20a1b50b8c4fff5`, validation `fbe1838e424c167ef34765d44706bbf54f7f0182c698580c7366c76270efd6e7`, build `ff15a44ab00a456f9d763f3ebc9e37d9e0cf915c0ada522c2b85deb7d5d8c9b4`). The source-account and empty-value projections cover the two observed HOME behaviors, not arbitrary executable initialization; the first alternate-scope attempt was overridden by the inner permission scope and is recorded in the question log. An independent reader returned `VERIFIED_SCOPED` for the copied ranges, analysed HOME scope and exercised handoff paths. It regenerated all 146 ranges, ran 15 analyser/permission tests, four Agent HOME tests, four skill HOME tests, a nonincremental TypeScript check and the source-version Shell CLI driver; it did not run the 265-test suite, build or compiled CLI drivers. Main independently matched all seven file and plan hashes named in the review and re-captured identity `43de69b6`. The reader identified a classifier regression-test gap: the existing HOME-change test also changes the decision from ask to deny, so it does not isolate the HOME-equality recheck. A later main-only regression keeps both decisions at `ask`, changes HOME inside the classifier and verifies one interactive approval, denial and no file effect. The assertion was tightened to use a declaration-prefix command containing `$HOME` and an unrelated content deny, so both actual samples still ask. Eleven HOME-focused tests and `npm run check` pass at `be4b70a3a903ba6487954ebe984aa7e30ddf51f9a7d46d1e0ff71821c38744f6`; only validation identity changed to `0552349fc18cddf5584cdb2f978a65568a2ab7673369f063b4048069826dde35`, while product and build match `43de69b6`. This added assertion was not part of the independent review. Main additionally reproduced the reader's conservative Bash observation with real `executeSkill`: HOME absent and an account-directory deny allow `/bin/sh` but deny both `/bin/bash` and `/bin/zsh`, although `/bin/bash` observes empty HOME in the separate shell probe. The Bash false denial and arbitrary-executable HOME model remain open; the scoped verdict does not assert their equivalence. The Bash probe SHA-256 is `cd884a1312a1b86e34a4547224ef3ce5a5130cc50133eb1213b017bef12ea6fb`. A second owned `executeSkill` probe sets HOME absent and supplies local Bash `BASH_ENV` and zsh `ZDOTDIR/.zshenv` startup files that assign `/startup-fixture`. With `Bash(printf /startup-fixture)` denied, both `/bin/bash` and `/bin/zsh` still run the declaration-prefix command and write `/startup-fixture`; `/bin/sh` is the empty-value control. This demonstrates a third initialization value bypassing the two-projection check, not a claim about other startup files or full shell semantics. Probe SHA-256 `ed9d2700e713ab4e03f0fdd548e7dd65f06fd9f7fd78422e24580113b00d7826`. A separate six-case compiled CLI fixture confirms both the startup-file misses and the missing-HOME Bash false denial, with `/bin/sh` controls and the zsh account-directory denial; successful cases make one owned loopback model request and blocked cases make none. Its SHA-256 is `2dedc0f73614b34780beb4942a400ca77c0c21f1697300691ecd34a7bbb69f34`, with unchanged product/build and overall identity `7f09b7ce`. The first fixture attempt produced an empty expanded prompt for the empty-HOME control and was invalid; adding ordinary instruction text allowed all six observations to complete. The source's selected Bash tool routes through `Czr` to `ale` [3506526,3518705), SHA-256 `071acadeb5780fbdf43feabe33e923971e8e5a63e6c76a7e17b77773addda188`, which builds a provider command and spawn environment. The provider `zgn` [3497474,3500269), SHA-256 `38ae241b5e8f19311c923bb15b55cc5115b31e3345012330afc58778ac8e6f0c`, may source a shell snapshot before evaluating the command or use a login shell when no snapshot is usable; source `Rgn` [3492127,3494603), SHA-256 `797014504538a0a2e06fa044594f3318d5ac9f43fa9afcb03240003b60a52f2f`, builds that snapshot by launching the selected shell with a login flag and the inherited environment unless its explicit no-inherit switch is set; `Nor` [3520891,3521162), SHA-256 `460bb265bebe0c046399b5560d923344fefb344fdf490650002dc457d9b578bb`, combines inherited environment with provider and sandbox overrides. All three ranges use the verified `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` identity and UTF-16 coordinates. The separately located `Qt` environment scrubber in `chunk-x0dc37w9.js` is invoked by the policy helper, so it is not evidence that ordinary skill Bash strips startup files. In the verified `ale` body, the separate `sxs` env override at offset 3516143 is conditional on `scrubCredentialEnv` (`Ve`) and Bash; source `sxs` builds credential-preservation names rather than establishing a general startup-file scrub. The Shell `call` obtains those constraints from `s.remoteCall?.constraints` at offset 4338649 and forwards them through `Czr` [4347622,...), which supplies `scrubCredentialEnv` only when that constraint is truthy. This establishes the local call-site condition, not the complete construction of every remote constraint. Source `sZe` [4329121,4330427), body SHA-256 `cbca7fd74f8ec36487063970c15393ff08944e6ba054302579761954d0d9a52e`, gets `remoteCall` from the tool-call context and `sessionEnvVars` from the bound host context; `iye` forwards its existing context to the tool. The cited ranges do not show a local-only call injecting the scrub constraint. `Ss`, `chunk-j41p6s5m.js` module SHA-256 `b5b2bc8fd8ceb2339e3d4abcb5f3548e896ee99b9889591bd14e492e12225856`, [24261,28470), body SHA-256 `28156239828ea18e0a7fa2c293e8ebde4afc20348fa590dcc0ae10be25981b4c`, returns `process.env` unchanged on its no-special-case branch; otherwise it creates an environment copy and applies multiple filters. This does not prove the complete filter outcome for every configuration. An independent source reader verified this chain and corrected the field comparison: source does have a `shell` field, but `Chr`, `chunk-zm79vshj.js` SHA-256 `671868b11eba6ba55d389827d959adb062c3e427d2a4f8ebe00d4f0755d6763c`, [10920,11169), body SHA-256 `4da25fdbcef681a5eb7123f1e5603b9845c2a10a70ccd8aced01ef5003eae8eb`, admits only `bash` and `powershell`; other values fall back. Those values select a source tool, while host `SkillInfo.shell` accepts an arbitrary executable and spawns it directly. The reader also traced `SMr` [3491129,3492127), SHA-256 `f72090dac062e6ded6606e0394a7d22a1fc8cf269fa365188afcf9230fc040bb`, `_Mr` [3487377,3489749), SHA-256 `fdf371c30919bc5ba5ae6f6def227cb9e2e6f189043beef3e89f7df7bfd013dd`, and `kMr` [3489749,3491129), SHA-256 `b6d9f16c3d9aefe0421b3ccdba78f83f6915990ebe2f60e298a45a7f125bd423`, in the verified `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js`: the snapshot's explicit state serialization writes shell functions, options, aliases and PATH, with no direct exported-HOME serialization. Serialized function or alias bodies are themselves arbitrary shell text, so this observation does not prove the sourced snapshot can never change HOME. `Z` reads `os.homedir()` in the analysing process, without reading the spawned shell's final HOME. The reader did not run the recovered application, establish Bun's HOME behavior, or audit every session/sandbox override. The arbitrary-executable host environment/approval contract remains open. As a possible host adaptation, the source `D3e` [3256051,3265763) routes an unanalysable command through early checks before returning a `too-complex` ask at offsets 3257391/3257456; `MTr` [3230062,3230922), SHA-256 `bf08d08abf4d7f7554e3e4cbfd86fe6f9a08f6a36f8b70e7594f64e4542f5377`, first checks literal/prefix denial and additional safety predicates. The existing host uncertainty path can hold execution for approval, but it does not yet implement every source early check. This mapping supports investigation of an uncertainty decision, not a claim that arbitrary executable initialization is understood or that an approval reveals its final HOME. A parser probe distinguishes a direct `$HOME` expansion node from escaped and single-quoted literal text, while `${!H}` names only `H` and `~/file` appears as a word; parser-node name matching alone is insufficient to identify every HOME-dependent command. A direct decision probe with `Bash(printf /startup-fixture/file)` denies the direct literal command but denies neither bare `~/file` nor the quoted literal `"~/file"` under bypass. A separate local shell-only probe shows that bare `~/file` expands against `/approved-fixture` in `/bin/sh` and against the startup-file value `/startup-fixture` in Bash/zsh. This combination is not an end-to-end skill observation. The host skill paths have different approval surfaces: model-invoked skills and the one-shot CLI call `executeSkill` without `requestPermission`, whereas the interactive path supplies a one-time callback. Any uncertainty hold must cover direct and transitive HOME use, startup-time changes after approval, unmodelled shell syntax, and unconditional deny rules. A shell-only fixture confirms that querying effective HOME by launching Bash also executes `BASH_ENV` and creates its marker, so such a probe is itself an effect before approval. Another owned zsh fixture keeps the same `ZDOTDIR` string while retargeting its link from an empty directory to one containing `.zshenv`; the new startup marker runs. Thus an environment-string snapshot alone does not pin startup-file bytes. On this host `zsh -f -c` skips the fixture's user `.zshenv` while plain `zsh -c` reads it; system startup files, other shells and the compatibility impact were not evaluated, so that flag is not yet an implementation contract. `bash --noprofile --norc -c` still reads the fixture's `BASH_ENV`, so those flags alone do not fix the Bash case; the source and host startup chains do not establish a complete classification yet.

An additional owned `executeSkill` probe uses an explicit `/approved-fixture` HOME with the same startup file and deny rule. `/bin/sh` writes the approved value; Bash and zsh each write `/startup-fixture` despite the deny. A fourth Bash case starts with `BASH_ENV` absent and adds it in the later `Bash(true)` approval callback; the previously approved first span then writes `/startup-fixture`. Thus pinning HOME alone leaves both present-at-preflight and later-added startup environment cases open. Probe SHA-256 `4631bcdb4fa515feea3669abc509cb50fae6cf1d381418425a1168969de168bb`; it uses `executeSkill`, not the compiled CLI, and cleans its owned files. The observed shell behavior is local to this macOS host. The two existing skill HOME binding/control tests also pass at `9c67d17e`; they do not cover these startup-file cases. A direct shell-only control with explicit empty HOME and the same startup files yields empty for `/bin/sh` and `/startup-fixture` for Bash/zsh; this control did not exercise skill authorization. A separate noninteractive shell-only `ENV` probe with an explicit HOME preserves `/approved-fixture` in all three shells on this host; it does not establish behavior in interactive, login or other-shell modes.

Unknown-HOME probe: passing the source marker `__TRACKED_VAR__` as the analyser's `ht` value makes plain `"$HOME"` and `X=$HOME` vectors too complex, while the independent `$X` example still yields `printf old` and `printf new`. This is not a complete unknown-value representation: `"prefix$HOME-suffix"` yields the simple candidate `printf prefix__TRACKED_VAR__-suffix`, and an input deny `printf prefix*-suffix` matches that marker as if it were a value. An assignment to another declaration can likewise leave the marker in `envVars` and generated text while later `$X` candidates remain useful. A literal `__TRACKED_VAR__` also occurs in legitimate source text, so a blanket substring check would conflate it with a marker. The source `Z` [68158,68694) and `it` [61892,64542) distinguish tracked values through `Vs` [3597,3648), all in the verified `chunk-zjewzgfh.js` and UTF-16; the host's Boolean sampled-rule projection does not carry that distinction. These in-memory copied-analyser probes show why substituting a third magic HOME alone would neither close the runtime gap nor preserve deterministic denials. An additional probe places `printf "$HOME"` before the declaration: under the marker, `E6` stops before it records any declaration-prefix count, so simply catching the unknown sample also loses a later deterministic `printf new` denial. With HOME after the declaration, some deterministic candidates remain visible, but not every ordering. A proposed object-coercion sentinel also fails to model the plain quoted HOME read: `Z` returns the non-string object and `it` passes it through before any coercion, so no callback fires. For `FOO=$HOME env`, the copied assignment parser accepts that object as a partial assignment and creates `undefined=undefined env` instead of an unknown result. A non-string sentinel therefore violates the analyser's string contract. A direct host decision probe with a nonmatching HOME deny and bypass mode returns a readings ask for Bash indirect `${!H}` and `${HOME:-none}`, but allows a simple quoted `$HOME` when its chosen value does not match. These examples do not establish full coverage of indirect expansion or arbitrary shell syntax. The sentinel experiment therefore does not supply a viable attribution mechanism. The copied analyser also treats unquoted `$HOME` as simple for `/valid` but too-complex for empty HOME or a value containing spaces; choosing one placeholder would erase this distinction. No product change is made for these probes.

Skill-hook environment plan: source `UF`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [2969279,2980094), SHA-256 `b4b038af35a56452053fce6496b1f8da1f354bdbd8f802216550869db071a9e0`, builds the child environment before spawning and gives the same process to the configured-async or header-adoption owner. Its module digest matches the manifest. Source `qe` registers each skill definition separately as recorded above. The host preflights every skill hook before launching the child, so adapt that existing boundary by keeping the HOME observed at each approval in an invocation-owned map keyed by the merged event, original position and definition digest. Parent definitions receive no skill snapshot. Carry the map through foreground and registry children, including in-memory resume; never add it to session metadata. For a configured-async command, forward its one approved value into the registry-owned `runHooks` call; a header-adopted handle already owns the spawned environment. Regressions must exercise distinct definitions, grouped and identical parent hooks, absent HOME, both async paths, registry resume and cleanup. This is a host authorization adaptation, not a claim that source `UF` performs this preflight.

Path-chain follow-up: the manifest digests for `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js`, `chunk-zjewzgfh.js`, `chunk-qxmpnv25.js` and `chunk-2bcyxj9q.js` were rechecked. This is not the recovered wrapper result. Copy the command table and its exact lexical dependencies into an isolated source unit before connecting the path gate; bind filesystem identity, working-directory ownership, source exceptions and read-rule selection explicitly. A cat-only regular expression or a blanket hold would invent behavior for the other source commands and cannot establish this chain.

Skill-hook environment implementation: the host uses merged definition identity (event, position and digest) to bind the HOME read immediately before preflight. Skill-hook registry source follow-up: `Ce`, `chunk-x1hnhzq0.js` module SHA-256 `b4c4dd97d03ce3924bdc71ec87f3e8ab42114091cf4e292c532089fef95a2ecf`, [4081,6037), body SHA-256 `c0c697ccb5a03588ffc3315263dd8a0e9847f45e5a16f0f36b0fefa2513882d2`, stores hooks by session and event, groups by matcher/skill root and removes individual definitions or clears a session. Path-owner mapping: a TypeScript-symbol lexical census of `Xce` reaches 73 declarations/imports and 6,735 UTF-16 units, including session plan, scratch, tool-result, job temporary, project temporary, memory and user-configuration owners. This is an upper lexical inventory, not proof every dependency is needed for each local branch; remote-only branches remain excluded by the user's scope. Source `SDn`/`uTe`/`Yg`, `chunk-2bcyxj9q.js` [215024,215541), exclude project-settings directory grants from the outside-read fence and require every observed path spelling to fall within a retained directory spelling. `Xce` [242279,243972) then supplies the recorded special-file exceptions; `Rk` checks explicit Read deny before that fence. Host `confineResolved` also rejects absolute paths when no extra directory is observed and excludes `.agent`; those file-tool contracts differ from the source Shell read gate, so using it as the Shell predicate would change behavior. The source `vX` treats `<&` targets in `ucn`/`dvr`, but the later read-block pass directly selects only `<`; preserve both stages when the chain is connected. `bL` [3159888,3160842), `uvr` [3159412,3159652) and `cvr` [3159652,3159888) already exist in the unconnected inline-recursion source unit with `c3e` from the matcher. Path extraction table scratch follow-up: the static lexical closure of `b7` contains 17 copied ranges, including `tf`, `dw`, `o3e`, `r3e`, grep/awk extractors, git option parsing and pseudo-device filtering. Host handoff follow-up: `Agent.authorize` can evaluate the same call again after an auto-classifier or `PreToolUse` rewrite, and the `PermissionRequest` callback can change the filesystem before it returns. The root-to-tool context is constructed before the call loop; model hooks and registry/skill wrappers create or forward their own contexts, and wrapper permission callbacks may run after the inner Agent check. Any integration must retain every applicable snapshot at each check, key it to the actual context workspace and call while accounting for logical/real root aliases (on this host `/var` resolves to `/private/var`), and clean abandoned entries after denial/cancellation without letting a fresh resolution replace an earlier held snapshot. `carryForkPermissions` copies permission state but is also used for saved journal forks; its caller alone does not establish that host calls share the source `ln` project-view lifetime. `send_peer_files` separately resolves each file, rechecks a resolved-path Read decision and passes the resulting identity into its opened-handle check; its staging path is a distinct owner and the six-case ordinary file-tool observation does not establish a defect there. This is static host mapping, not an integrated fix.

This leaf check does not replace the source stash or execution-time path validation; a leaf replaced after the check and parent-link changes remain the separate approval-to-use gap. In a local macOS filesystem fixture, `rename` over a terminal symlink replaces the link itself rather than writing the linked target; this single observation does not cover ancestor replacement, platform variants or the complete source `Xr` publication chain. `checkPermissions` for write, edit and notebook edit stashes the resolved input path before later policy and input rewriting; read, glob and search use the read lane. `GIr` checks `holds` and consumes the saved identities before `t8e` consumes the read spelling set, and only obtains fresh macOS identities when no approval-time read set was held. `Tzr` at offset 4330452 is the simulated sed edit consumer and calls `mY` before combining approved and newly observed spellings in its write path check; the ordinary Shell call only consumes its recognized sed target before execution. The copied stash therefore needs a call-specific context across these wrappers and a root/fork owner, not a mapping to the host's turn-level file checkpoint. The host's execution-time `resolveChain` and `verifyIdentity` do not by themselves preserve an approval-time set. Open cleanup adapter boundary: an owned in-memory probe `f2e461c6f0eed5b6a448249483b3ff9d717e8fc539207d3cd14b808a6fa4ca75` wraps the copied `QHt`'s supplied `Pe` handle and catches every `close()` rejection, under `node --unhandled-rejections=strict`. The aborted, late-arriving handle no longer terminates the probe, but a normal successfully returned handle also suppresses its later close error. That blanket wrapper therefore changes the live-handle error contract and is not accepted as production adaptation. A second source-adapted scratch probe `f5a35c13a89d1bd78075ea3eb59d0e3e3b3a6db259c5a6cef90a483d7caa9eb3` adds a rejection handler only to the late-close promise. Under strict rejection handling it reports the orphan's close failure, preserves a returned handle's close failure and open failure, and skips opening when already aborted. The supplied reporting callback is synchronous and nonthrowing in this probe. This is a feasibility sketch only; the production owner, a throwing logger, nested `Z3` behavior and real cancellation have not been verified.

A bounded independent reader inspected the leaf-link candidate at `f6d0a613` and found no reproduced defect in its stated terminal-link scope. The reader ran the three focused groups and 12 compiled CLI cases, checked the `sNe` plan and call order, and identified an early-denial semantic change plus uncovered ancestor-loop and future missing-workspace boundaries. It did not regenerate the plan, verify the original artifact digest, run check/build, or run the 84/169 suites and approval-retarget driver. A follow-up owned Agent fixture `ba3c929936b5329a20ae9b0bfae23e83fcefcd33add736bcfdbcd9accb88f179` confirms the early-denial effect for a U+FFFD path: the local write is denied before its configured PermissionRequest hook can rewrite the input to `safe.txt`; neither hook marker nor safe file is created. The copied source `di` returns a resolved observation for the owned missing U+FFFD name, and source `ek` in verified `chunk-wh2vcbh8.js` SHA-256 `4f11ef8e1e1749cb96813db9575a40fad7d44576d313172aaa372c4baf330b68` [4200,4269), body SHA-256 `1e8c5321688ec55960f00972cb8cca3b4bac45bd3e283739f4f56899504787a6`, calls `et` and checks round-trip equality. An independent static follow-up traced `c_`, `Al`, `xBo` and `n2e`. `c_` denies an unresolved landing via `Al`; `xBo`, `chunk-fyhzvvp0.js` SHA-256 `1392b254e378432c7206e82a258512a740c26ce57263941ba3be4eb279675d19`, [8410,14040), body SHA-256 `9eb3fe5a4110c74df42f7e8b8de9eaa42c3229a0769e6ccf21d56d79691abef1`, returns an existing allow or deny before the PermissionRequest path. Its `n2e` [15475,16947), body SHA-256 `547937869c3d431cf7360a71ca09ef93456219a3cb5d5981a96f77a43a068ef6`, rechecks an updated input. These ranges are zero-based half-open UTF-16. The reader statically derived resolved/ask for a missing U+FFFD path and deny for an ancestor loop, with an unreadable ancestor depending on whether a link was followed; it did not run the complete source gate or hook, or exhaust its rule and safety predicates. A separate host probe shows that both `leafWriteDecision` and `contentRuleMatches` invoke `resolveChain` and reject the U+FFFD input. `contentRuleMatches` already did so at HEAD before the leaf adapter; without a policy the new leaf adapter introduces the same early rejection. Thus the tested configured-policy hook failure is a host/source candidate that predates the leaf adapter, not evidence that the adapter alone caused that exact fixture. Both paths need source-backed reconciliation while retaining the existing execution-time `nonCanonicalText` contract. The bounded correction uses source `di` for the leaf adapter, with its filesystem owner injected from the host workspace, and supplies the same observation to file-content matching within a single permission decision. Rule roots continue to come from their original layers. All host rule buckets also retain the host walk's additional intermediate spellings. This preserves both a pre-existing two-link denial and an allow rule's requirement to cover the intermediate name; source-only matching changed both in new failing regressions. The additional walk is a separate compatibility observation and is not an approval-time snapshot. An unresolved write observation follows copied `Al` ordering before `sNe`; calls without an applicable content rule still observe the path for the write leaf check. The host file tools retain `resolveChain` and its execution-time restrictions. The platform facade, empty-ask-hook rewrite, direct invalid execution, explicit target deny, link loop and isolated-child rule root need focused tests and an actual compiled CLI check before acceptance.

An owned isolated-child fixture `4f08f73abbe1b0ef2ad3bf0a93eeb3366ef0d0a570cb1fc11e2ff1ac4c74a246` separately checks two inherited CLI-style rules. In the child, `alias.txt` links to `protected/data.txt`; `Read(alias.txt)` denies, while `Read(protected/data.txt)` permits the call and the returned result contains `PROTECTED_FIXTURE`. The parent has a distinct ordinary `alias.txt`, so this demonstrates the local matcher resolving the input in the rule workspace, not the actual child workspace. Source `Ui` [225036,225815) in verified `chunk-2bcyxj9q.js` resolves unanchored rule patterns relative to `se()`. In verified `chunk-qr9z1wer.js` SHA-256 `7c4094d82563f8ebbee3405c7ba3cdb48d5eb7701e274c48c111eb1aefc587a3`, `Wm` [2037,2082), `Aon` [2228,2274) and `se` [2355,2405) read an async-local cwd with a global fallback; their body digests are `34bf7bb0279243361840a15c43d258e060c08c7a3f7b48d0232ec43fce5a3fb5`, `5b4266176e6ac85ccf4f1f3affc566018073f76421b1f5fb7fcba022b40b6cb9` and `f342a46b7698adf67a68754e434b41d76b68b1942aafbed4cb32db7333a4ecf2`. Isolated-child construction passes its chosen cwd to `withProject`, but the complete call path installing this async-local scope remains untraced. Source `gRt`/`Mre` [217195,217458)/[220553,220592), body SHA-256 `336f6e3e13dc068e4e3ccaa830c1bf0e20b6e96879f8cdddb792d5b468e129c3`/`9151d678fadd5f148d86da05135d07cfb179ec238e56397b4bb66f981cf26f87`, choose the rule origin; source project/fork cwd ownership must still be traced before adopting a source-equivalence claim or changing the integration.

Leaf-link integration candidate at `f23cc2b9bd68e1af639499ff2eda07c901ef01a8b1132605fd944fb3ea631c56`: `sNe` and its formatter are generated from eight source ranges in `W385-leaf-link-source-plan.json` (plan `40186f33`, source `693e5767`, ledger `7c653e6a`); Node `stripVTControlCharacters` supplies Bun's unavailable ANSI builtin. `resolveChain` records the source hop's leaf bit; the host adapter `eddf3766` sends it to `sNe`, with a direct `lstat` only when a link loop prevents the host resolver returning its observation. The adapter preserves a prior denial and uses the actual tool workspace supplied by Agent, skill and registry callers. Three new groups cover direct decisions, PermissionRequest rewrite and an isolated child whose parent and child resolve the same relative name differently; all three pass. The earlier 84 file/permission/notebook tests and 169 Agent/registry/skill/restriction tests pass; check/build pass. The compiled CLI driver `37d67012` passes 12/12 cases across the three write tools, ordinary and ancestor controls, leaf denial and rewritten input. The first two groups and the 84/169 suites predate the final child-only test addition; product and build inputs remained unchanged. Inputs `c7adc5bd`, validation `5e7b5a23`, build `9555238b`; test `e65d3966`. The later bounded independent implementation review and its uncovered approval-flow difference are recorded above. A later six-case `Agent.run` fixture `bad0a3e29844ec444ca8264f1189bbb2e720c7c0a77f355dd0afdba25f042630` confirms the remaining ancestor-link gap at that identity: stable read/write operations reach the public file, direct protected paths deny, but retargeting the approved parent link changes a fresh verdict to deny while the actual read returns the protected content and the actual write overwrites it. The new compiled CLI baseline `scripts/smoke-approval-retarget-cli.mjs --baseline` independently reproduces all six real file effects through a PermissionRequest hook and owned loopback gateway, at `f6d0a613949e9540c2c50db8bc80099fef16f7872af8fde6b02a779ed8adbd26`. Driver SHA-256 `8b2719832fd3eccfeb9c0de6139e96922c7b54f78f1a9e8f6385326d385ceeab`; inputs `e7ebab6b` include only that driver added after `f23cc2b9`, while validation `5e7b5a23` and build `9555238b` are unchanged. The check does not bind approval to later execution. On this macOS host, the source `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` value `536870912` opened a direct file and returned ELOOP for a symlinked ancestor; `/dev/fd/<directory>/<child>` did not support file creation or directory reads, and `/proc/self/fd` was absent. Those finite observations do not establish other host behavior.

The compiled approval-retarget driver now covers six tools and 18 cases. With `--baseline`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `notebook_read`, `notebook_edit` and `read_media` all reach the protected fixture after the approval hook retargets an ancestor link; stable links reach the public fixture and direct protected requests deny. The image case checks the actual returned image bytes, while the two notebook cases inspect the returned cell or written notebook. The driver SHA-256 is `f69651f255da6e045c5a7da9cbb82a4573a19b331e45534b9076e3c0b6dc653e`; content identity is `4fb07ee20b2a3adb4a89b17662047cfc678cb29d82ef8eed36107e07179cfc23`, inputs `605881806481729218158cb15573ead7cede1c121d33412febc50ad221eafc54`, validation `5e7b5a23cf811e1a7b4a6393b948996f676e3d6767e08983f1d8ce5ffa069dcf`, and build `9555238b0cafc59428cd73bd69dabd3fb53f2e66033b846ab862b747c99b24aa`. The driver change is the only product-input difference from `f6d0a613`; this is an observed defect baseline, not a passing repair.

An owned interleaving probe exercises the copied `eMt` with its scratch Node `QHt` facade. The fixture replaces an already-approved physical parent with a link just during `open`, restores the directory before the copied post-open spelling check, and observes `PROTECTED_FIXTURE` from the returned handle. Repeating the same interleaving with the source macOS flag substitution, `(flags & ~O_NOFOLLOW) | 536870912`, yields the source read refusal. Probe SHA-256 `88090cd314f9a1b901c4c90b1b35426ff3cfd4156bc563d26cab86aa4343ca38`; this is a deterministic injected interleaving in a copied-source fixture on this macOS host, not a production exploit test or a cross-platform result. The scratch facade's ordinary `fs.open` is therefore insufficient to reproduce `QHt`/`Z3`'s platform contract. `QHt` [8336,8667), SHA-256 `4568cb3d75f575298d440671755068ed3f1a873496e33cf3dc5e7b815884cea7`, and `Z3` [8920,9027), SHA-256 `7d54b7a05061db1bb64868692ac751f4f9ee8bff6ea84a74f49fbc537841fbb5`, use source `ai` and `zut` from `chunk-8pet3bvp.js`, module SHA-256 `aff6bcfc21f2613576b751f7d19d89c0059fc4eb1ae6af3a60381555a783bcf4`; `Z3` first tests `wGn` [3034,3275), SHA-256 `afce3d145ff56377c135d8e078bc65a1a2a006896ee013646062b78848f726fb`, then substitutes `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` for `O_NOFOLLOW` when available. The three `QHt`, `Z3` and `wGn` source bodies are now generated in `src/path-open-primitives-source.ts` from `W385-open-primitives-source-plan.json`, with explicit filesystem, flags, state and capability callbacks. Plan SHA-256 `4e0cfe5d029a7797e0e4365f9169db23fe5e8889ecceabd6e7b4714371648aee`, generated source `18567a3e259ac46c2b6e27300404d777b414bda820c8ca0507cf3f3d7bfe1b1c` and ledger `02b14e6036886feccdee116da7a12c91467acb76b954c765d7632d801834fbc9`. Two source-state fixtures and one actual macOS filesystem fixture pass 3/3; they cover successful and rejected capability probes, cached outcomes, platform flag selection, cancellation and late-handle closing, plus a stable file and an ancestor link. Test hashes are `278f8164af946ea118b2649625bc63921d55f7f5921f6f1f9e51da4e0a512e76` and `cd566f58d35ceeb379cb5e6761211860ea5564a05477d8c3aa1e63bd6ebf4a34`. Identity `f6290b2e77dd7f706fbc52c05b8a5fb93cb664883de1d6e184bce01a6fa1b461` binds inputs `b21a9b3e4af0a941bb73a4872d765d83afa6aea73a8a75e106f5f0bf1c76f9d5`, validation `a977b23be89648ec74852b321ae74a57e339cbeda89f7785c85e41ff8e22b3da` and build `4a79839b20cb428653a4c9810e0d9ce81ca2d2f5cabd84bb1f2ba2e37163f752`. A separate compiled helper injects an aborted open whose late handle's `close()` rejects; the copied `QHt` calls close once and emits an unhandled rejection (`close failed`). This is the exact copied-source behavior under an injected failing close, not evidence that normal local handles fail to close. The production host must account for the reported failure rather than assume successful cleanup. The callback facade is supplied by the caller, this unit has no production caller and has not received an independent review. The helper is not a CLI acceptance run.

A temporary 77-range identity factory was generated from the existing verified copy plan. A later assertion joins the copied `di`, `Be` and `mY` against an owned retargeted two-link chain: repeated observations retain only the original requested spelling, and the new landing is outside that intersection. The two identity groups plus stash tests pass 5/5 with check at `12a21c752ae3396cace80c8f967ab5723de393853cd80c7fce18e88fee5e9669`; test SHA-256 `04ce7f587a0561c7cf62571e6a879ec7459ac17f6e0fe4b296bd59f2f789f9ca`, validation `b070ed673fcf383f81b9c1eea4b76382af8c51ef4a465800d1cafe33984dc87f`, inputs and build unchanged from `40c644f2`. The identity factory now additionally copies source `tl`, `chunk-ae84tp6z.js` [11681,11730), SHA-256 `a1ef7566d730b862d332c82c4c742d5856c8edf590571e100039461e32205a71`, and `Vut`, `chunk-8pet3bvp.js` [3464,3525), SHA-256 `7ea0f6b644fc0dc677717cb7e355edc647a0761fbd2ff903b896ad8ca5fa0086`, and exposes them with `ywe` and `UMt` for the consumer factory. The 79-range identity plan is `a3db27e4da137c6e55a2d48a32e2ebdcb7b8fc4e3c4c374b5c13202515f1f3ba`, generated module `630d22100ef23d51fced9cacbb6549222955766f392ec4c304a6fbb000b053bf`, ledger `d06f482a2e3490e9b3e2d8810a0da0c706a8f3558c9d68f18b8a6d9c36b9c274`. The new `W385-approval-binding-source-plan.json` copies 20 slices from verified `chunk-bf3z2ftn.js` and `chunk-886tf6ja.js`, including `eMt`, `US`, `Xr`, identity fallback, descriptor and error helpers. Plan SHA-256 `f0c6fc6473894e9ad727ff2e6cf88810c374c18e4315dc4a274b06c7b9677679`, generated module `5ab100ac3529996a4e226c1074063385b57dd8b61aa9fb936c8fee19d0f463e5`, ledger `7c521bc940b02895997d4b1e737b0462748c00ea7fb4a68d0140b033c9700dda`; all 20 bodies match their original slices. Its factory injects platform, constants, filesystem facade, opening functions, logging and file decoder. The decoder is a supplied test callback, not the source decoder port. Two macOS-projection groups exercise stable and retargeted reads and pins, and the read identity fallback for the same inode, different inode and missing identity. The combined identity/stash/open/binding suite passes 10/10, check/build pass, and a compiled source-unit helper reads the stable file then raises `SymlinkReadRefusedError` after retargeting. Test SHA-256 `fa87d97607a741a7db7a5a12667291e653b9d04bf609bb6ebf6b74ee2a67535a`. Content identity `e1c1fbb957031212841129d863009f8c3862718c9363ce98dacb923665167f70` binds inputs `81cb7543cf09f8e0ae6147967c758dad71f15275d57806f0f458bb1a78538aca`, validation `e7ac3a440d19f56707b49f2ce766bd29fb3b76224778731f9f2f659ff61708cb` and build `4a40e089aa4d275f0eaebeb83f30f5608a27a8a7f8317c338177d1035c797422`. A later assertion exercises `US` with `createParents:true`, checks the created nested directory and confirms no file existed at the requested path. Two binding groups and check pass at `34deaa0d705af2949654ee7204b33d2403e247f0da9dac1936886396e5c6584e`, validation `fdc6e48f693d8ca6de36fb49d0b4ed4532574376b36d7457d41ea20e2c543c2a`, test SHA-256 `3640745226dd6e5319e887072a4061a5a2db5bc1261ed9fe6e8a576e4839110d`, with inputs and build unchanged from `e1c1fbb9`. A separate owned macOS fixture `626570fa1f59beacdab73e8d88d1aaf929897e5261f98ecd24c339423cd58ce3` creates and closes 100 directory pins; `/dev/fd` reports 13 descriptors before and after and a separate caller-held descriptor still names the original directory. This finite observation does not establish cancellation or every exceptional cleanup path. At that earlier identity the test supplied null launch ancestry and a simplified decoder, did not write through `US` or exercise authorization, and was run on macOS only. On the same macOS host, an owned filesystem experiment holds a descriptor for `public`, moves that directory aside, points its old pathname at `protected`, and renames `public/.tmp` to `public/result`. The held descriptor still identifies the original inode while the pathname rename affects the protected directory. A second experiment stops before the rename: the path's current inode differs and the source-style `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` directory open returns `ELOOP`. This demonstrates that a held fd alone does not anchor a pathname rename; it is an injected syscall sequence, not a claim that the product or the full source `sC` was exercised. The source `uno` calls `recheckBeforeWrite` immediately before `pPe` and closes the pin afterward; host `atomicWriteFile` compares directory identity before its `renameSync`. The interval after the recheck remains a separate publication boundary to model and validate. Source platform constants, real decoder, remaining platform branches, owner lifecycle and atomic-write adaptation need source-backed integration. Neither unit has an independent review or a production caller.

The binding fixture now uses the copied source decoder `lwe` instead of the earlier simplified callback. `W385-approval-decoder-source-plan.json` copies seven zero-based, half-open UTF-16 slices: `SPe` [781,1212), `NRr` [1361,1520), `jGn` [1710,1902), `lTo` [1902,1945), `WGn` [2181,2308), and `lwe` [2923,3214) from `chunk-0hm793yn.js` SHA-256 `68ae2b9259693643a3c539efe830c1ca70ca542d02f41faeafc3975784cbaed4`; `ID` [80736,81001) comes from `chunk-gyf58rwf.js` SHA-256 `7a6a01732760c89b5d434cc5ba98faf72b10917d85b771671f8201e9e3d8d2ad`. The plan records each body digest; all seven generated bodies match their original slices. Plan SHA-256 `a584c6e0211fbc9191aa37dd67f0aff23cfed2fbecb696cf706454e49a460ba0`, generated module `18cb10565395fe49f381318fdfc82842fe3d4143ef2718ca150cdb93d1e444da`, ledger `9589a234824c1999a191a7a0a4e61ebb7da7d1dcde072cbecba5ecb4d034be9b`, binding test `b34747ddc215c658b8b3fae72b558e3d230ea32875c32e32cbd8f7f8c9b8b92a`. The fixture covers UTF-16LE BOM, CRLF normalization, lossy UTF-8 and the file-size limit. Ten identity/stash/open/binding tests and check/build pass, as does a compiled UTF-16/CRLF decoder helper. Content identity `0d09ca4c1b138996b1074b0c694e45077085a0169a443b442f2c11439f24db8f` binds inputs `5a392bd29e9ee54586757753350e73c7a7d789d03b8f2722d4569be687d1ba60`, validation `9a47ffd92c7ca87dfd2e471777b4c55fbeb8662c0be7330c4371e9fd751b6e13` and build `cd6474986f5ecfc6a0afd6a3e513493729d03ccb81a787c4c079cbd1d1b07aab`. The macOS fixture still injects filesystem and platform ownership, does not write through the pin and does not exercise authorization. A later independent review returned `VERIFIED_SCOPED` for the four source units and their recorded per-file hashes. It matched all 109 UTF-16 slices and module hashes to the extraction manifest, regenerated four plans and matched all eight outputs, and ran the identity, binding and open tests 7/7 on macOS. It independently reproduced the already recorded late-open `QHt` close rejection as an unhandled rejection. Its binding fixture assumes macOS `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` works; unsupported filesystems, non-macOS branches, launch ancestry, production authorization and actual writes were not verified. The previous overall `0d09ca4c` identity could not be reconstructed from the subsequently changed tree; per-file hashes were verified, and the later current identity was `adb0e3ad`. The production usage chain remains open.

A separate owned macOS interleaving fixture `d44f20e87bad80ef1d306cb47a5b49869b1d4248dd03e34c17f6abd3634409a8` composes the copied `US` pin with the actual compiled `atomicWriteFile`. Replacing the pinned parent before `readExisting` produces `SymlinkWriteRefusedError`. Restoring the parent, awaiting `recheckBeforeWrite`, then replacing it before invoking the host writer leaves the approved file unchanged and writes the protected fixture. This is an injected gap between separate calls to copied and compiled units; it is not an execution of the complete product or source writer. The evidence rules out calling the existing writer after a standalone pin recheck as a complete approval binding. Publication, retained temporary contents and platform-specific directory anchoring need a joined source-backed contract and explicit tests.

A separate owned interleaving runs the actual compiled `notebook_read` tool with an ordinary approved path. Immediately after its pathname `stat`, the fixture moves the checked notebook and renames a second notebook into that path; the returned cell text comes from the replacement while the original remains in the moved file. Scratch driver SHA-256 `25ac9e57e0c5ec4d898c3d5864546d73eace9df2cfc72ae897c03f2e2128abc8`, compiled notebook module `659617c228de40a567af601259b3034cc7251ae82bfd0454526ec729cf2134d8`, `node --import tsx` exit 0. This injects a precise host-tool interleaving, does not run the recovered sample or a compiled CLI session, and is distinct from the previously reproduced approval-hook retarget. The source generic `Read` notebook branch `QIr`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3552396,3553150), body SHA-256 `01a1a8c4abfd296271389c01dffb6b50f2ede38d9ae5befef677713ae80b9c43`, uses `chn((await ke()).handle)` and stats that same handle; its caller `bhn` obtains `ke` from `eMt` and closes it. `chn` [3529036,3529719), body SHA-256 `5512da00b747d1d4068a5dc2e6fa93a799e2d81821d86c477e59167dcaa0ce46`, checks the handle's type and size and calls `ID` on that handle before parsing its bytes. Coordinates are zero-based half-open UTF-16. A second compiled-tool interleaving, scratch driver SHA-256 `b566127a2204b0dbda9c2f2b4431a69b9caab480d6bd9d26b1b9c542704bc9d9`, performs the same swap during `notebook_edit`: it edits and publishes the replacement, records the original pathname in the history callback, and leaves the moved original notebook unchanged. This fixture injects the handoff directly and is not an actual CLI test. The recovered NotebookEdit body already cited above consumes the write snapshot, pins with `US`, and reads through the pin before publication. A complete host notebook integration must consume approved spellings and read through a checked handle while preserving its existing parser, history and output contracts.

An owned deterministic interleaving against the actual compiled `atomicWriteFile` moves the public directory after the staged file is closed and substitutes a link to a protected directory before the writer's identity check. The writer refuses publication and the protected file remains unchanged, but its error names the old temporary path, which no longer exists; the complete staged bytes remain in the moved original directory. Scratch driver SHA-256 `77dc222209a2194f5c5c747682462dd578eee57905a2329207ab45e5a3f8e6f0`, compiled writer SHA-256 `0c540e83c4ea7fb0ef80b61b034caacb7dd079966b673e16abace158bb3ab0c4`, exit 0. This uses an injected `closeSync` boundary, not an observed ordinary scheduling race or an actual CLI tool call. It establishes that the current kept-content error can report an inaccessible old pathname after movement; a publication adapter must retain or report recovery evidence according to its own verified contract.

A separate descriptor-relative publication experiment on macOS arm64 used an owned C fixture, SHA-256 `f4f4714f3623dc9771313886c5a5dfb265f609f9be385401851e43d3bca81c9f`, compiled with `clang -std=c11 -D_DARWIN_C_SOURCE -Wall -Wextra -Werror`. It opened an approved directory with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW`, staged and measured the content with `openat`/`fstat`, replaced the directory pathname with a link to another owned directory, then called `renameat` using the held directory descriptor for both names. The moved approved directory received `NEW` and the replacement directory retained `PROTECTED`. The fixture cleaned its files. An extended version, SHA-256 `48d4e6d8ffc0a957fe20860abd98b65c534e3a6634a2180e5d2a04c645fff289`, also forces `renameat` to fail with `ENOENT` and then verifies that the staged `KEPT` bytes remain accessible through `openat` on the held descriptor before cleanup. This establishes temporary-file retention in that syscall fixture; it does not establish a usable recovery pathname after the directory has moved. A separate macOS C fixture `a1634c8d8ca34d4a2690db74a40834f43f355aa2601368e61c99c23fe9e56ee6` observes that `fcntl(F_GETPATH)` on a held directory returns its new pathname after rename; after removal it still returns a pathname but `stat` of that pathname fails. A second fixture `e9434de044832052047932fded2e68a4a262e77f4473f64bd1d28cb622bb02a7` stages `KEPT` through a held directory, moves that directory and reads the retained bytes by joining the `F_GETPATH` result with the staged filename. This succeeds while the new pathname stays stable. Recovery-path reporting still needs to verify the returned path's identity and handle concurrent movement or removal; these fixtures do not establish an always-available retained-content path. These are deterministic syscall-level observations; they do not exercise product authorization, package installation, error-message preservation, the source writer or other platforms. An additional Node probe found `/dev/fd/<directory-fd>/file` returned `ENOENT` on this host, so that spelling is not a substitute for `openat`/`renameat` here. Node `v26.10.0` did not expose `fs.constants.O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` or `O_CLOEXEC`; the installed macOS SDK defines them as `0x20000000` and `0x01000000`, respectively, matching the values observed in source `chunk-8pet3bvp.js` SHA-256 `aff6bcfc21f2613576b751f7d19d89c0059fc4eb1ae6af3a60381555a783bcf4`. The source `wGn` capability probe [3034,3275), body SHA-256 `afce3d145ff56377c135d8e078bc65a1a2a006896ee013646062b78848f726fb`, is UTF-16 and remains a runtime capability check, not a universal platform guarantee. An additional Node fixture `28937506aba46faa89ce6abb68b44c036a35216baa55aafb041efb17db98b03e` opens an owned real directory with those numeric flags and gets `ELOOP` for its symlink spelling. It tests this host only and does not establish how to publish through the descriptor from Node. The current Node file API and macOS peer-identity addon expose no such publication operation; implementing one would add a native package/build dependency and needs an explicit source-adaptation contract and fail-closed fallback before product integration. The existing `scripts/build-native.mjs` builds `peer.node` only on Darwin and skips when headers are absent; `package.json` ships that compiled addon and its C source, while `smoke-package.mjs` installs with `--ignore-scripts`. A second native capability cannot assume install-time compilation or Linux coverage, and reusing the peer addon's optional load failure as permission to use a pathname writer would leave the publication boundary unbound. Any adapter must make capability absence and the portability contract explicit and validate the packed artifact.

A five-case direct comparison of the copied `di` and host `resolveChain` on owned macOS paths found matching recorded spellings for a normal file, one ancestor link and an ancestor dangling link; both retained the same landing there. For `two -> one -> target`, source `di(two/file)` returned only `two/file` and `target/file`, while the host additionally returned `one/file`. The copied `di` uses its Node filesystem facade and null launch ancestry; source unresolved loop handling also differs from the host's thrown `ELOOP`. A separate compiled host decision with `Read(one/file)` deny rejects `two/file` under those conditions. The source path gate has not been executed for that rule, so this is a spelling difference and host observation, not a claim about the complete source verdict. Approval snapshots must preserve the producer's exact spelling contract; silently substituting the host walk for `di` would change intersections and policy observations.

Hook/stash source follow-up: a bounded independent reader traced source `yk` [3357795,3359265), SHA-256 `9b674e8e26ef5275e417f8c3149231312d8be22e1cc000b95c2c508004da5a27`, `Lpn` [3357036,3357557), `f65374f118391f80e0d2767049bf04c38da8db3cf17b7b5aa0f40098596ce687`, `_Wo` [2237279,2239655), `b5af0ffa2181db2da644e010f38119b64568ad9458c2dc58473cea792004b4d3`, `KWo` [2275902,2308030), `6059f8425fe5233c21ae1d2e13b92ff63c8415643bcea9a61cc8b54294b3e002`, and `sxr` [3317869,3319984), `a9ac4c828abe9c0c18d1499df3011ac3dd6c5565fb09d7f9bcc340f62bc7bf4b`, in `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` SHA-256 `4c74137eb23221981404d0ca87b765912f83344851d5c132b21857e5685b03da`. `yk` invokes tool `checkPermissions` on its parsed-input path, with earlier denials and the `Lpn` ask branch able to return first; `Lpn` also invokes `checkPermissions` on a rule-ask path. `_Wo` passes updated input to `yk` before its final allow, and `sxr` rechecks a PermissionRequest updated input, with an ask converted to deny in the observed branch. All cited ranges use zero-based half-open UTF-16 units; the reader's results are static and did not execute the sample.

Approval-to-use observation at candidate `43de69b67c3a3a9ae894d5d232c8fd6cc3a8927a3708487e71910913d8314e89`: an owned `Agent.run` fixture uses `Read(protected.txt)` as a deny and asks for an `alias.txt` link initially targeting `public.txt`. With a stable link the read returns `PUBLIC_FIXTURE` and the write changes only `public.txt`. When the approval callback retargets that link to `protected.txt`, a fresh permission decision is `deny`, yet the approved read returns `PROTECTED_FIXTURE` and the write overwrites the protected fixture; direct requests for the protected file are denied without an approval callback. The six asserted cases run real host file tools with a scripted provider, not the compiled CLI or the recovered application. It confirms the same read/write effects and both controls, with two fixture model requests per case. CLI fixture SHA-256 `90e90e3f7142132f7a7d3c480a4a552388ff3742b733cc30c3cdb381fa9f2227`; product, validation and build identity remained `43de69b6` after the run. Only created fixture paths were removed. These observations confirm an open host approval-to-use gap and do not settle full source equivalence.

Host owner follow-up: source `cXe`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3539900,3540938), body SHA-256 `0d9c34cf1dc35f1edf2b1cb5bd3ef69b4c642a09e2888e106ea69848d5922ea3`, forwards the read tool's session and the current invocation's tool-use ID through getters. In the same module, the inspected Read, Write, Edit and NotebookEdit producers stash the `di` spellings before their path-rule decision. Their complete `async checkPermissions` text ranges are [3542972,3543340), [736116,736494), [4161813,4162223) and [4177027,4177228), with respective SHA-256 `36e5da1ebb05048f5ea911a9bcff8d38bca50ee538625fd2dffe40b25aa6f956`, `108f11eae7129333dccae0d8959afc84712174a19eba878aef338c16b3be1abb`, `0e18017c40b5b7bd5c9174ab728d8a981269db1ebc472d63f48ebebe138dc3cb` and `f8e624c8ff4c72fcea8f534a91616578f9d55ef172c7ca6ed6108c1caa20e5c2`. The existing complete consumer ranges are recorded below. `Age` [2253028,2253305), SHA-256 `442903adfb8d7cb177d3383660eea7a47b1cc30449f73fe90fa4b40f90de9970`, records a checked input/result in per-tool-use pipeline state; `UJo` [2254035,2254243), SHA-256 `e39b7c7e700f609ef286b0a96767d3004276bf53b82c6cec56c9c3fe9acdbb0a`, replaces that pipeline input/result after reprompt. Both use the same module and UTF-16 coordinates. The module digest and the `Be`, `mY`, `eMt`, `GIr`, `bhn`, `uno`, `US` and `Xr` body hashes were rechecked against the extraction manifest before this follow-up. This is a static ownership observation and does not settle host cleanup or nested wrapper timing. Ordinary model calls have `call.id` during authorization, rewrite and execution, while model-hook tool calls have a separate `call.id` and directly create another `ToolContext`. Registry and foreground skill wrappers run additional permission checks before forwarding the same context; the current context carries neither a call ID nor a path snapshot. `carryForkPermissions` preserves a permission-rule owner, which is not the source stash, while child sessions separately inherit live permission state. The peer-file tool has its own per-file check, resolution, identity verification and staging path, so its attachment reads need a separate mapping rather than an assumed ordinary-tool handoff. The direct model-hook read lane performs `permissionDecision` and constructs its own context before executing, so it needs a call-bound observation even though it has no interactive permission callback. The registry's `beforeTool` can perform another approval after the outer Agent decision, and foreground skill wrappers make the same kind of late check; a snapshot captured only in the outer Agent would miss their final authority. `send_peer_files` authorizes each input in `readPaths` and separately stages concurrent files, so the source aggregate-key producer cannot be replaced with repeated same-ID/lane `consume`. The current `atomicWriteFile` obtains a directory identity after execution-time resolution and checks it before rename; it does not by itself bind that directory to the earlier permission decision. The source writer's `US`/`Xr` uses saved spellings and a held directory, and `eMt` opens a checked file handle; a check performed only before calling the current atomic writer would leave a separate handoff interval to account for. Host `notebook_read`, `notebook_edit` and `read_media` also resolve inside their execution calls without a saved approval-time set; notebook editing uses the same atomic writer, while media rechecks the path and open-handle identity within its own read. The source notebook consumer at offset 4179657 takes `mY`'s write lane before `US`; source attachment helper `ONe` [648276,648512) allocates its own `attached-read-` ID and stores read spellings plus macOS identity, while `PNe` [647820,647920) invokes `eMt`. These notebook/media host observations are static, not reproduced cases. The source root-sharing statement is specifically the `ln` project-view factory, `chunk-4bw62nzm.js` [53565,56759), SHA-256 `b75f89bf46e94a0b107b6ee1c4cfbc17e7cda431839a6b7fadea0b1ea4d305f5`: its `withProject` creates `kind:'fork'` using `m.root` and shares that root's stash. Source callers include workflow (`chunk-hedqf2m1.js` offset 68905), a child context (`chunk-qw2csqy5.js` offset 22617) and an isolated-agent context (`chunk-wj6fycw8.js` offset 34337); their module SHA-256 values match the extraction manifest. In `chunk-wj6fycw8.js` SHA-256 `ce38912bf9ddb32271235f9639bd42afb9c42c11ce66ee1d4471362d4a99d8ae`, the isolated context's `project` is passed to `Bn` at offsets 37904 and 39087. `Bn` [50955,51005), body SHA-256 `4ec375c06330a78814d7a07ef40a5be1dd94dfd56144ead9ed388e85ab364963`, calls `bPe(project.cwd, callback)` when a project exists. The previously recorded `bPe`/`Wm` bodies install that cwd in the asynchronous scope. These are UTF-16 coordinates and demonstrate this scoped source caller, not every possible child or diff-view path. This is not enough to equate the source project view with every host `forkSession` or reopened journal. Source-equivalent sharing, ID namespaces and cancellation/recovery cleanup remain open. In particular, the host's content-rule matcher can call `observePermissionPath` on its own when no observation was passed. A future producer must retain the same observation that determined the rule outcome, rather than resolving again only after a callback, and must distinguish checks that returned before a path observation. The final consumer still needs its opened-handle or held-directory contract; merely storing the final resolved string is insufficient.

Multi-file source boundary: `SendUserFileTool` in `chunk-169gwk6m.js` SHA-256 `20274e176c47ef5093ba9fc58f415045685c29c0eab6f46b9b118384fe5765df`, `checkPermissions` [13493,14897), body SHA-256 `ecad5cef75869ea16d391bffcc5233f4291053c3288fc54cfabc8657d801ac26`, checks each `di` observation, stashes their combined spellings under a deduplicated/sorted file-list key, and saves per-file identities. Its key function `j` [15641,15684), SHA-256 `7e882bf80ff5e7e78f0f76c413b6bb9bcfe414420a50841f6f8e61ca3bb1ea82`, calls `D` [883,919), SHA-256 `e08913e4ba564b11551e01e87af4d4ba5f0fde1d9cce2dc2407afcc87d3df2bb`, in `chunk-3qabb19b.js` SHA-256 `ceb8c8490f39408310286cc14cacb1998192c8c34a80724882f988431f1bf5f2`. Its consumer `M` [15716,16172), SHA-256 `1d1c73891e45a47b65de1687787064369ae91df9a73dc2a9aced73a4e476ce3a`, takes identities before consuming the aggregate and distinguishes expiry from a missing snapshot. Coordinates are zero-based half-open UTF-16. This is source evidence about that producer, not proof that the host's local `send_peer_files` shares its remote or user-delivery semantics; the host separately authorizes each `readPaths` item and stages the files concurrently. A single ordinary `Be.consume` clears all entries for the same ID and lane, so repeated per-file consumption would lose sibling observations. Any local multi-file handoff needs an aggregate or independent child keys and a tested effect/cleanup contract.

Launch-ancestry follow-up: in `chunk-k1419ccf.js` SHA-256 `c16ef15d854983cef4a918f0e03680314c24bb195dfb9771dad68fc7d8b6ef1a`, source declarator `c` [893,1016), body SHA-256 `ba11aac42f155c35a10ce5727fd97037eda94ec94e6048501104dd5c3133492d`, captures process cwd at module initialization; `qpt` [1016,1148), SHA-256 `08308ef410fd7527d6e0f6f8befdfc05d6910168bbd8fe3c209da44fc1b0d42d`, returns null when that capture failed or the source's session-kind environment marks a background session. Both intervals are zero-based half-open UTF-16. Source `ywe` computes a shared ancestor against its current filesystem owner's cwd. A synthetic no-link fixture `aedff1f843421a8e52f910f9e9a8061b1ff7b47b87afb6192b19d96c22b85cd7` compares a denied ancestor with explicit launch cwd versus null; both observations match in that narrow case. It does not establish equivalence for links, protected ancestors or the complete filesystem facade. The host currently injects null, and a matching owner for startup cwd versus workspace, background launch and resumed child has not been installed.

Attachment boundary follow-up: an owned `Agent.run` fixture, SHA-256 `e90d43c0908c60ce6a58a622166aa398dbc85932081347e454354d7a9aecc5b4`, sets `Read(protected.png)` to deny. Its direct `read_media` permission decision denies the protected file and allows the public control, while the user explicitly supplies the protected image as the `Agent.run` attachment and its bytes reach an owned in-memory provider. A compiled CLI fixture `a650a98f64ff0a9d23a5ca34a7c80c740f3b630d28c705af973cd011445dd195` separately runs `--attach` with a public and protected PNG under the pinned deny, checks the saved session policy and confirms that both image payloads reach its owned loopback provider. Both local runs exit 0 and clean their fixtures. This demonstrates separate host entrypoints, not a judgment that explicit user attachment must share model-tool rules. Source `ONe` call sites in `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` at offsets 3680893, 3697556 and 3698072 belong to automatic reread and file-reference flows; `PNe` also serves a generated edited-image attachment. Source automatic reread and file-reference flows first call `u2` [3706559,3706810), SHA-256 `56d5531489a05c3b62f445a3e0eaf93b1759e1b3d0104aff509fb454beb3424c`, which checks network paths and the source read-deny gates; restricted flows additionally use `_U` [651646,651728), SHA-256 `3e1861b051f2a513418edf6594b1e00e9c3e08ce814261a10e40c6099b341c60`, before `ONe`. These ranges are zero-based half-open UTF-16. Those excerpts do not establish the contract of the host's explicit `/attach` or `--attach` entrypoints. The approval integration must map the producer before applying model-tool semantics to user-selected media.

Call-ID ownership follow-up: an owned `Agent.run` fixture starts two concurrent `Session.open` sessions against the same workspace. Each provider emits `same-call-id` for `read_file` on the same path, and both calls succeed with their own result. The same fixture passes those IDs and paths to one copied `Be`: the second stash intersects the first and one consume removes the shared entry. Fixture SHA-256 `4db7a6c232c3900488b701158c888cf27ff2c3a6563809f26acfd9ab480e1df3`; command `node --import tsx $CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp/w385-call-id-owner.mjs`, exit 0, with the fixture sessions and directory cleaned. This combines real host calls with a separate source-stash demonstration; no production stash is wired. Host `assertPaired` and model reply validation enforce uniqueness within a pending batch, not across sessions. Therefore a root-shared integration also needs session/call-scoped keying, or evidence of a stronger upstream ID contract, before it can safely use raw provider IDs. A second owned `Agent.run` fixture, SHA-256 `a3587f40d0520ad216377f1fddb08060150fcdabb3b147b5c39eb16b5ee6da7e`, runs two turns in one session whose provider reuses `reused-call` for the same file; both tool calls succeed. Its separate copied-stash control shows that an abandoned first entry and a later matching ID/path would intersect. Thus prefixing only the session ID does not distinguish such sequential calls if an entry survives denial, interruption or a skipped consumer. A separate copied-`Be` lifecycle experiment, SHA-256 `81b1698680605d29b6c460af67c3bf848daf3417e206e17c2693cdd592ff8571`, assigns a fresh internal key per invocation and clears both lanes by consuming a deliberately absent path with that key. It checks same-provider-ID overlap, an abandoned read/write entry, sequential reuse and a surviving entry after bounded eviction. This is a finite in-memory feasibility test, not actual Agent integration; source `Be.consume` clears matching live entries, while evicted and poisoned states have their own bounded semantics. A follow-up expiry fixture, SHA-256 `410f0f7fb0ddc95ef6fdd186a91a2ee3ce91f8a946922719f93dc1d41c2a40e0`, confirms that consuming an unrelated empty-path key does not clear a different evicted key: its first `mY` lookup raises the source expiry error and the second falls back to fresh resolution. An additional copied-`Be` probe with caps `(1, 1)` shows why that cleanup cannot be adopted as a complete lifecycle: after the abandoned old keys have been evicted, `consume(old, '', 'read')` leaves their path-specific eviction markers in place; after another eviction the lane becomes poisoned and a never-seen key returns `j8e`. Production defaults allow 1,048,576 markers, and this finite reduced-cap test is a design boundary, not an observed production failure. A later in-memory probe, SHA-256 `91925a81e6f527c6909fb0a0bdb42b3516ee0f0d2d52ed3e3fa232828a07a9bb`, supplies separate invocation tokens and marks a wrapper retired before deleting its matching live and evicted keys in both lanes and its read identities. With caps `(1,1)` it preserves an active sibling, returns `j8e` for an evicted active call, clears abandoned markers, and rejects use of the retired wrapper. A separate saturation case confirms the copied lane's `poisoned` bit remains true after retirement and makes a missing fresh lookup return `j8e`; clearing individual entries must not clear that uncertainty. This probe directly manipulates copied `Be` fields and is not an integrated owner, does not solve global poison recovery, or prove all cancellation/concurrency paths. Any production adapter still needs explicit retirement of live and evicted entries, error and read-identity ownership without allowing a later lookup to replace an expired check with a fresh resolution. Existing model-hook and child wrappers add further namespaces; source `withProject` sharing does not alone prove host-wide ID uniqueness.

The source read and write consumers trace the saved spellings further than the earlier `mY` fixture. In manifest-verified `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` SHA-256 `4c74137eb23221981404d0ca87b765912f83344851d5c132b21857e5685b03da`, UTF-16 half-open ranges `GIr` [3546500,3549105), SHA-256 `248d14a0ff792119fb7be53262c5d35ffb9ad5e323c6a78693939e47171e4513`, and `bhn` [3551998,3552396), SHA-256 `2c79f2764c9eb72f5cf68f2f145a1e5b13a6222b767b2f0423fee8c3c63fc4a3`, pass the read lane into `eMt`; `uno` [739599,743280), SHA-256 `df63c9e2f785bb91b417b3e28077126194204b62b3455dd8a0185b3f83f6e4a7`, and `sjr` [4167188,4171835), SHA-256 `0b015d911c07b5fb182d1834aecb9a2c4eff7541ff84b4243a75c4bc23bfadea`, pass `mY`'s write lane into `US`. The latter wrapper `US` [15698,15966), SHA-256 `32ff0dc923bce008300752bb0a971987748655924cad9c3d3a05d491c723896f`, retries `Xr` [15966,20119), SHA-256 `c8e47181c7df00dc3eec1b3e8ee08d7f3d7f2a059231ef7d83d65a0df8fb54d8`, in the previously verified `chunk-bf3z2ftn.js`. `Xr` compares current spellings to the approved set and pins or rechecks the write target with platform-specific branches. `uno` invokes `recheckBeforeWrite` then `pPe`, `chunk-f606a53w.js` [7111,7501), SHA-256 `cb02006e3659cfde2fd6c47719410b11a2c1d579cebaba0dff2f1b5d3b3c06e6`, which calls the source atomic writer `sC` [12501,15618), SHA-256 `06461197d24df8eb6df4a9f03abae92c64c4b23a1648bef272ebf86623aa6364`; platform-specific I/O paths determine whether the held directory participates in publication. The local writer has its own content-preservation contract, so this source path still needs an explicit port/adaptation decision. The source `sC` stages through a sibling name and, after a completed temp write, may attempt in-place fallback for `EXDEV`, `EPERM`, `EEXIST` or `EBUSY`; it also attempts fallback when an existing target's temp creation gets `EACCES`. The `ST` literal in `chunk-1t033v1j.js`, SHA-256 `07eb5f690bc8609b43708ba2a3d6b2f17bfee11bc493f965342ba8fe7e84cc6e`, [3029,3076), has body SHA-256 `46ead56e572d14ef84ca574132928183a1c7373eeb421296fb71fb480eb21a8f`. The fallback opens the target and checks hard links before truncating it; when `refuseHardLinkedInPlace` is absent, `GGn` [3076,3115) calls `$Rr` [2949,3009), which returns false for served calls and otherwise calls `be` [2926,2949), returning false. Their respective body hashes are `989526e675204cf2658b141f74e3a5f3ac816c03c837dc6be546cb63759bf77d`, `10824c6c2a057d4444612bba86129a34a6e9381188c68487b2ccb6a2ae590490` and `824bbb41fd0526aba516e860c5eef8e939145a5bac194629836b3f261ae901e2`. An explicit true override still refuses hard-linked targets. After a failed fallback write the source reports a retained temp path, whereas the existing host writer reports a retained temp after any fully staged publication failure. The separate source synchronous writer `tTo`, `chunk-f606a53w.js` [9543,12501), body SHA-256 `a115505313f8d4cde6eb42c096c94cb925b0d52d319d8478f91e1fb9adf8586b`, uses the same fallback family. Its preserved staging file is named by an ordinary path, so held-directory publication and retained-content reporting are host adaptations rather than copied source calls. Source `i1e` [6196,6285), body SHA-256 `d1bc7d4160fbb1e02ad98b8a3e5a0707eb1aff803aef340d5767e4ce2fe50152`, treats `EPERM` as unsupported sync or chmod in addition to `EINVAL`, `ENOTSUP` and `ENOSYS`; the host's fsync helper instead includes `EOPNOTSUPP` and omits `EPERM`. The source fallback, hard-link feature owner, retained-path behavior and this host's error contract must be reconciled before replacing the writer; this is a static comparison, not an observed production failure. The host invokes `context.fileHistory(real)` before write/edit/notebook publication; `Session.touchFile` appends a workspace-relative history event, while automatic checkpoints capture separately at turn boundaries. A descriptor-relative writer still needs a stable reported path and the same pre-effect history order; the descriptor fixture alone does not test that contract. A separate scratch composition copies `le` [10266,10484), `ee` [15021,15155), `eMt`, `US` and `Xr` exactly; all five output slices match their source bodies. With the existing copied stash, consumer and path-identity fixtures, a Node filesystem facade, `macos` platform adapter and null checked identity, four owned-link observations pass: stable-link reading and write-target pinning succeed, while retargeting after the saved check raises the source read/write error classes. The fixture does not write contents or execute actual authorization. A second four-range copy adds source `et` [11435,11479) and `Ft` [11626,12246) to `le` and `eMt`; all four source/output bodies match. Under a Node filesystem facade and a macOS platform projection, four cases cover stable reads, retargeting to a hard link of the same inode, retargeting to another inode and the same-inode case without checked identity. The same-inode case reads the original approved path; the other two retargets raise the source read error. This exercises the source identity fallback with caller-supplied device/inode and does not establish real source authorization, platform alias behavior or concurrent post-open correctness. Builder `9ef4bb336b2477b5144d2ddb4f0af4f7b64778ccac27cbe55278c3a9a1bc0bf5`, plan `5b14526c27b8450463eebe222af12a14cb49c0bf9e9029bc90a5651fe04f3654`, module `18ebe0854f6910f9252ca2b4a6f041e93f50b1c21d381f88b298a1ba73f3d8e6`, ledger `d3b4aa100345336957f9f72948a3a8a21f20a15e8e6939de8d9114666f2af79e` and probe `45fc14434698715a8d226928c88f08ee556d6548ce16c2c7b827589f9df88458` are in the job temporary directory. Platform aliases, new-path branches, real owner lifetime and all host entrypoints remain open. First fixture builder SHA-256 `e574cf585b5a488df562e143f7679f38920ae977a17fc969e08b8edc8ba9c634`, plan `33b7f8dfe746fedaf08c1f4cc7f32c703913cfaf627aab4afa82aacd7df90011`, copied module `95c26108d3d77330154d7945d9938e73aee7237a5f27340564a2de26ed4bbb78`, ledger `2274483e7d93019c733c68235ab2fd537a7acc00c699a37fd4caa50d613c776e`, and probe `ce239b789acf186264922ff6ae8e3082a639371cf27b04073265a61d29e5d0c9` are retained in the job temporary directory.

Path-identity follow-up: manifest-verified `chunk-gyf58rwf.js` SHA-256 `7a6a01732760c89b5d434cc5ba98faf72b10917d85b771671f8201e9e3d8d2ad` supplies `yo` [67735,68107), `ff` [63574,63768), `He` [64745,67735), `di` [73816,73857), `qn` [74330,74593), `er` [74593,76645), `wpt` [76729,76820) and `hxr` [76853,77042), all zero-based half-open UTF-16. `igr` [942509,942809), in `chunk-qxmpnv25.js` SHA-256 `ff90ce1457de715318c0e75b0aa15798ca20ac7f836bb6d61be502dfdc1ce218`, passes a successfully canonicalized read's landing spelling to `Rk`; when a canonical path also came through a link and any deny bucket is populated, original spellings are added for deny lookup only. `Rk` checks these deny spellings before the unresolved and read-block branches. `Wd` [216784,217195), in `chunk-2bcyxj9q.js` SHA-256 `fac54023446990731332f837b388955e357036b2797a11f93150648afb45f7e0`, additionally maps the macOS `/private/var` and `/private/tmp` aliases and has explicit case and UNC options. `resolveChain` records all traversed spellings and the existing file-tool confinement checks each; using that rule unmodified for the Shell source read verdict changes canonicalized inward-link behavior.

An owned nine-range path fixture combines the copied source gate with Node `realpath` and exact synthetic deny callbacks. Seven cases pass on actual temporary files and links: direct inside and outside files, inward and outward file links, an explicit deny on the inward link, session grant and project-settings grant. The copied gate admits the inward link's inside landing; the host walk records both the outside requested path and inside landing. The explicit synthetic deny on the requested link wins before the fence. This is a conditional local observation: the `yo` adapter uses Node realpath, `di` is supplied, exceptions are disabled, and unresolved ancestors, globs, link races and full Shell policy are not tested. Plan builder SHA-256 `2694086427958b5880ce2641947fca2fbae6c5a48e3c8eeb25dfe887c555e66c`, plan `244ce065da7f98d12a39c83e45a8c979aa83efda62d2d1954d2cbb3cce4fa972`, fixture `f99a7673a0b1b6c6762b28703455a0691d74c5b178140f94729d0320e37e5c2e`, ledger `f90e6e4133a01b9f78ba85cfe2f9ba873cf7c39c7eea1bbd9cf37a210fe875b0`, and probe `2cc1bb8f6c28f6bb2619d04f553549fc49c95477bcc89098322327072a9c470f` reside in the job temporary directory; scope scan reports zero unresolved names. The files and links were deleted by the probe's own cleanup.

A later owned identity fixture copies 77 manifest-bound ranges for `yo`/`di`/`_o` and their same- and cross-module static dependencies into separate original module scopes. Its filesystem facade is an explicit Node adapter and launch ancestry is supplied as null. Syntax and undeclared-name scans pass, and all 77 output slices match their source bodies. Eight actual temporary-file cases cover ordinary inside/outside files, inward/outward links, a missing descendant, an outward dangling link, a link loop and a missing descendant through a linked directory. For the dangling link, `yo` retains the requested spelling as noncanonical while `di` retains both spellings and the outside landing. For the loop, `di` returns unresolved with both link spellings while the host `resolveChain` throws `ELOOP`. The fixture does not establish launch-ancestry, platform, network-mount or concurrent-replacement behavior. Builder `c7c1ecbe2ef3ee13bf4bbc6cd476eafa8d29889a791ac781416866f561ecff27`, plan `367a726ae6a920ef5bd57b177aba41b280cbb523312d678398f27cf141607f84`, module `d3bc7c5c838f940591ff9d38eee2de16958543a57f86cff4e8fef74615af2cd3`, ledger `d222eb424dcf44cf7b5fd91ca0be1a27174f828ddb00191903f1fa30236bfb0e`, probe `d223a0fbe272ebc55da1c46f3326ae9f8168ffaf31a048bc6bb280a7f21c29a0`, all in the job temporary directory. The first scratch plan collided on the distinct source-module name `Rt`; its separate-scope replacement passes both scans.

A second owned fixture joins that copied identity module to ten copied path-gate ranges. Eleven real-file/link cases pass, including outside and dangling-outside holds, inward-link admission, session versus project-directory grants, a loop hold and explicit synthetic deny precedence for the loop's second spelling and an inward link. Directory membership, deny-rule lookup, exceptions and unresolved-path message are supplied fixtures; this does not establish full source policy or production behavior. Its builder `8559e0491d4ef8c63987baba584330798d29878bbaabda679597104c770d49bb`, plan `dbc93d8d7fbce37102c2f08c50647c4c9decfd6c8b55d2ccbad273e8df2c6207`, module `33e49484a0fcc7ebdbc1bda40bbfcbce264293e55884345239baedceacee64f3`, ledger `62ed14973b55381706e844e8895b07dcbd3406f0e824e06a4a92a7090438e5dc`, probe `8a3e6fce10b247b0fc0254ed1c6565c9a172a113655dc3b6e24efeb3653c8bfc`; all ten source slices match, and syntax and undeclared-name scans pass. Both probes clean their own files.

A third owned probe joins the copied analyser, wrapper, command table, `nvr`/`icn`/`Zwr`, actual-identity gate and filesystem facade. Eight conditional cases pass: direct and three wrapped outside `cat` inputs ask, an outward link asks, an inward link passes, an inside input passes, and an explicit synthetic deny on the inward link denies. Extra-path, glob, directory, deny and exception callbacks remain supplied, so this is not a complete Shell or path-policy result. The current host has distinct authorization consumers. `Agent.authorize` uses the live session state, `executeSkill` clones the policy and rebinds the live owner, its foreground fork uses an adoption-only child plus a pre-execution `approve`, and registry execution performs parent/scoped decisions before an optional `beforeTool`. The outer skill child deliberately has a bypass policy. Therefore a new path gate must receive the owning workspace and live directory observations at each of these call sites; placing it solely in the outer child policy would bypass the skill-specific owner. The source `outsideReadsBlocked` reason is bypass-immune and classifier-disabled (`trn` [987,2081), `$pn` [3360771,3364305)), while `Agent.authorize` routes any `floor` to manual approval. The complete host projection and allowed explicit approval/rewrite lifecycle still need verification.
