A separate owned compiled skill-child probe checks the approval handoff in both foreground and background registry execution. Each starts with synthetic HOME `/allowed-fixture`, configures an ask and a sampled deny for `/denied-fixture`, and changes HOME to the latter from the single skill approval callback. Both commands write `/allowed-fixture`, make two scripted provider calls, and return a successful tool result. This is explained by the child Agent's outer authorization pin being passed through the inner `beforeTool` wrapper; it does not exercise an outer PreToolUse environment mutation, changed policy, restart or unrelated shell runners. Probe SHA-256 `ef27202db25afc2d0a8babcf1a0b5f0563bec9eacd48a1080bc7cf5cabe45dab`; the owned files and registry are cleaned in `finally`.

Skill interaction also differs by entrypoint. A slash skill invokes `runSkill` with a user-permission callback in `src/interactive.ts` [3867,3875), while the one-shot CLI and model-invoked skill pass no such callback. The initial outer fork uses the bypass child policy, and the inner wrapper performs the originating skill's decision after its own PreToolUse handling. Any read-block integration must account for both the pinned process context and which owner may present an approval. No source equivalence is asserted for this host arrangement.

## Candidate closure, static only

The 71-function/74-declarator selection plus `pyr` was reconstructed into a separate scratch file using the existing `scripts/copy-source-plan.mjs` generator. The plan binds each of 146 source ranges to the verified module and its own digest. `node --check` accepts the generated JavaScript and the existing scope-aware scanner reports 175 declared names and zero unresolved names, with `./parser.js` and `node:os` as imports. That original scratch file has no parser implementation and is not a runnable fixture. The separately generated fixture copy and its bounded runtime observations are recorded below. Its `wd` and `$Ts` are placeholders for inputs, not established product adapters. The scratch wrapper's `setOptions` stores those values globally; that wrapper is suitable only as a static candidate and is not an accepted session or process ownership model. A static call inventory finds `ht` as a home-directory read and no direct `process.*` operation or dynamic import; this inventory does not establish dynamic property or callback effects. This initial static review did not execute a recovered source body; the separate owned fixture execution is recorded below.

Scratch plan-builder SHA-256 `57c256cd65675670582f9a117552297d595ee80c60bd5510d72c5d2dfb83f3ab`; plan SHA-256 `d9964d29a74f5847be2fcf349c01be6724324ac96c4d2879772eb18f130de18e`; generated file SHA-256 `92938829988f216563d3449e542da04c4175672c5324036a19ca48b8be36ed24`; generated range ledger SHA-256 `61e585dd7163367d84c9dfc9347ecf6abb597e23b9d0f3da3948cc9a22d0ad23`. A second bounded static reader verified 146 unique ranges, their source order, each range digest and copied text, and the module digest against the manifest. It identified the missing parser import and the unresolved `wd`, `$Ts`, and `ht` adapters; the reader did not run the candidate or prove dynamic effects. The host's foreground, background and skill shell runners all currently pass `process.env` to `spawn` (`src/tools.ts:675`, `src/background.ts:392`, `src/skill-runtime.ts:96`), so the host has no observed subprocess-environment scrub owner matching `wd`; its adapter must follow actual host state rather than copy a vendor environment toggle. The source `FTs` registers a feature reader at `chunk-bk5ct2gw.js` 530632 whose data comes from `getFeatureValueWithSource`; `$Ts` uses `true` when no reader is installed and also unless a default-host payload explicitly returns false. Its source storage is class `c` [1834,1908), declarator `f` [1912,1919) and registration function `FTs` [1920,1957) in `chunk-0yczyn9c.js`, with body SHA-256s `6fc48d2eb906363c96e0e37cb16a8477f6716298c2107a4d22dd8290bccc3686`, `7a0fbaa2f802e7b8ff9b6d0b2214440532d9684fb0c02b525eb329e9977f1e8c` and `7f23d5484664056af77b385df8109f357fcb2dabcc81c852773f8f4b54a727a4`. That supplies a source default, but a fixed true product value would be a documented local adaptation, not evidence of source parity under other feature states. `ht()` must match the home seen by the shell process for the call, including any HOME-affecting environment changes. A separate Node probe on this macOS host set two synthetic `HOME` values in succession and observed `os.homedir()` return each current value; it restored the original process environment before exiting. This establishes local Node behavior only, not a call-scoped environment contract across all product launch modes. An AST inventory of top-level function bodies finds the shared prefix state writers in `E6` (snapshot/reset/restore), `jt` (`L`, `M`, `H.set`) and `F` (`B`, `H.get`). The separate counters `A`, `j` and `P` are incremented by `he`, `Kt`, `v` and `it`; unlike the prefix state, they are not reset by `E6`, so a port must preserve their monotonic delta semantics and test repeated calls. These files are a candidate for dependency review, not an approved source port or proof of behavioural closure.

An owned scratch fixture now runs the 146 copied source ranges after independently verifying their bytes and substituting the exact `yyr` helper, the already-ported `vS` parser, `scrub=false` and `feature=true`. This does not execute the original policy chain or establish full rule-store equivalence. A separate compiled-function probe using the four-prefix full-line content allow reports host `allow` in `default` and `bypass`, `ask` in `ask`, and `deny` in `dontAsk`. These are host observations only; default-mode output agrees with the real CLI case above. They do not establish source behavior for the other modes. An owned `/bin/sh` probe compared synthetic HOME values with Node's `os.homedir()`: the values matched when HOME was empty or set, but with HOME absent the shell saw an empty value and Node returned its operating-system fallback. This is a local process observation and shows that assuming a shell-variable fallback from `homedir()` needs an explicit compatibility decision; the process environment was changed only inside the disposable probe.

The source clamp `fTr` uses `xX` for both exact and prefix allow matching after collecting raw spans and analysed command texts. A compiled host clamp probe with only `Bash(echo:*)` returns unmatched for `timeout 2s echo ok` and `command echo ok`, while accepting `echo ok`; no commands were executed. An owned six-range `x8t` copy and the compiled analyser each return the entire literal input as the only span/text for these three cases; the copied `T_` maps all three to `echo ok`. The `x8t` plan, copy and ledger SHA-256 values are `e155b076f1fcc0603d430dceefff658201ac9350eb979b8faa491941e7087d43`, `0ccfcf1ca7e5691679e306f615fc084f176b5ca8cddf6c502e188be4f4f517da`, and `bf702b1d562a142f3cd04eadd8373b2e6e4f24f0f8d1da0e37dabeb078b85bb8`. The copied `m_e` also leaves both literal spans intact; static `xX` then adds `T_`'s `echo ok` candidate, and its prefix branch accepts `echo` followed by a space. The copied `jxe` parses `echo:*` as the prefix `echo`. Under the recorded source rule and successful parser premises, `fTr` therefore admits both wrapped controls. An owned fixture combines the copied `x8t`, `xX` and compiled analyser and supplies a source-shaped session allow Map; it observes the exact or prefix match for every span of both wrapped controls and `echo ok`. The remaining source clamp context and final authorization are not exercised. Clamp candidate parity is therefore a separate follow-up within W385, and permission-sampling completeness must not be reused as clamp completeness.

The `shellReadings` completeness field has two product consumers: `applyShellReadings` in `src/permissions.ts` and `checkBashCommandClamp` in `src/agent-tool-restrictions.ts`. Source `fTr`, `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3207522,3208297), body SHA-256 `0225c17047b751f2a8de8c11eda3a1c86fe72973bd2e4d83fc7364c8ca3ecd17`, checks the first `E6` result for `too-complex`, then matches parsed command texts; it does not invoke `pyr` at that point. `x8t` and `W`, `chunk-m00pavz3.js` [2427,2524)/[3826,4317), obtain individual parsed command texts; module SHA-256 `e4dcdda42fdf15ff719179977326a414b62ad13d5992832859839c9203c16a62` matches the manifest. On the recorded candidate tree `W` traverses the four ordinary commands and ignores semicolons. `R` [4317,4474), body SHA-256 `d1c2dc5e9510acdd86027d026dc019a6b71fbc7e0ea5a8e10b295bbc5d6a2e92`, checks for nested subshells; the recorded tree contains none. The compiled host's `checkBashCommandClamp` returns `null` for the four-prefix command when its one group explicitly lists the full command and each of the four segments as exact `Bash(...)` rules; this is an internal compiled-function observation, not an actual child-agent CLI result. Therefore adding the prefix sampling limit directly to the shared `exhaustive` field would turn this host clamp acceptance into `unverifiable`, while the source `fTr` has no `pyr` branch. Source rule-store representation and actual child-agent integration need closure before claiming the same source outcome.

Product permission decisions are made not only by `Agent.authorize` but also on updated hook input, child-registry execution and read-only integrations. The local shell tools share this decision surface; `checkBashCommandClamp` is called separately before authorization in `src/agent.ts:565`. A host adapter must therefore attach the prefix sampling result to the permission decision path without silently redefining clamp completeness, and must preserve deny precedence, bypass behavior, user-approved calls and hook-updated-input checks. The existing per-session permission owner is live across inherited child paths (`src/session.ts:608`), while detached and isolated processes can inherit different environments; an unscoped mutable global for host options would require its own ownership proof.

Source `sxr` [3317869,3319984), SHA-256 `a9ac4c828abe9c0c18d1499df3011ac3dd6c5565fb09d7f9bcc340f62bc7bf4b`, rechecks a PermissionRequest hook's changed input through `yk` [3357795,3359265), SHA-256 `9b674e8e26ef5275e417f8c3149231312d8be22e1cc000b95c2c508004da5a27`. Its `dtt` [3317678,3317869), SHA-256 `2a6de563a40b1fa921011170dc00e7a2e522e26201ff7c87d5748379ecb70594`, forwards a deny or ask from that stricter check; `sxr` converts an ask there into a denial. `yk` intentionally filters some ordinary asks while retaining rule asks and safety checks, so this is not equivalent to denying every host ask. The host's hook-updated-input path (`src/agent.ts:690-703`) invokes `permissionDecision` again but currently blocks only a deny; existing `test/agent-lifecycle.test.ts:1323` covers an updated input denied by a rule. A source command expanded into a denied reading therefore must reach that same recheck. A later owned Agent probe SHA-256 `2de7b4e347420b53fbbf84d9a7aafe2c9a251625b720d9c3b44302232ed308be` confirms that a rewritten file input whose fresh host decision is `ask` with `floor:rule` nevertheless writes the new path. A four-case compiled CLI baseline SHA-256 `514d5596c92d1cf429ea56f4f926312689be02dcb9561e0c2904a086a6360632` uses default mode, explicit `Edit(original.txt)` and `Edit(asked.txt)` asks, `Edit(denied.txt)` deny, and a PermissionRequest allow. Rewriting original to asked writes `asked.txt`; rewriting to plain writes `plain.txt`; unchanged approval writes `original.txt`; rewriting to denied returns an error with no file. Each case uses two owned loopback requests and releases its session lock. Source `c_` [235473,238027), module SHA-256 `fac54023446990731332f837b388955e357036b2797a11f93150648afb45f7e0`, returns a rule ask before the plan and ordinary-path branches; in the non-strict `sxr` path a changed input reaches `yk`, `dtt` and conversion of a returned ask to deny. The source outcome is conditional on that branch, matching source rule context and the earlier input/safety predicates not returning first. Host `floor:rule` identifies explicit ask rules, whereas generic mode and incomplete-reading asks have different floors. A provisional host `floor:rule` guard produced the expected different-path denial but failed the existing replacement-character rewrite fixture: its tool-wide `write_file` ask still matches the rewritten safe input. The guard was removed and that previous contract remains. The separately added different-path fixture now asserts the currently observed behavior rather than claiming a repair. After removing the guard, 36 related permission/plan/child tests and check pass at `39b26a2d4417354ba1b67bd1ea9e4bab183e20e53488959b1e7f70ddc6030f02`; inputs and build match the plan repair at `f9afb2c4`, and validation SHA-256 is `33dbe4c5d98739c8cf07b06b2aa6bb2156f0f66648aae888840e215e781b483d`. For a source-shaped whole-tool rule whose name matches the source tool and whose `ruleContent` is absent, the read source `lc -> S -> qx -> P` selects that rule again; `Lpn` retains its ask unless `checkPermissions` returns a deny. `lc`, `chunk-nkb63p1y.js` SHA-256 `cb746f58859c7e9d52383597ff3a5c08bea091d17746697e713d379408504e5c`, [12757,12877), body `03f9b29bae06b6cf6474eb43745a9c50b14830ebce6b4d9a261d28628603e49d`; `S` [10712,10931), `f6c734b43d2f902e16e099ab0b531ff08deaac5c00372020e3a44d4f4f4307c2`; `qx` [9783,10178), `e16a13d3edc8c8e14ddde92366116327e7fe0c810bd5eac5edad6dd63ea0abe8`; `P` [8969,9414), `e9d3b92f226a163c4d22a3401987c0c501415f663c167f2d1f273dc4ff2a9e7f`; `xm` [8835,8885), `0c6d6ac28d005a894b2518692038be4ebfde937f57142cbdd0cca65ec10faeca`; `Lpn`, `yk` and `sxr` ranges above. These are zero-based half-open UTF-16 source observations, conditional on same-name source rule admission and non-strict hook execution, not an original-runtime test. They indicate a source/host behavior difference for the existing tool-wide ask fixture as well; the old fixture's blanket ask is not itself evidence of a source-preserving contract. To retain its original test purpose without weakening its assertions, use a mode-based ask for that replacement-character fixture, and add a separate tool-wide-rule rewrite case that expects the source-derived denial. The host `floor:rule` branch can then adapt only an explicit-rule ask on a hook-allow updated input; generic mode, incomplete-reading, unchanged approval and non-allow answers remain separate. The source sends a returned rule ask to denial in this branch regardless of whether the ask matched the original input; no matched-rule identity subtraction is introduced. `Agent.authorize` now maps the host's explicit rule-floor ask on an allow-with-updated-input to the existing error-result path. Six real Agent cases cover path ask, whole-tool ask, ordinary rewrite, unchanged approval, explicit deny and mode-only ask; the prior invalid-name fixture keeps all its file and hook assertions with a mode ask instead of the source-incompatible whole-tool ask. Thirty-six related tests, 70 Agent/classic-hook tests, check/build and two actual compiled CLI drivers pass. The six-case hook driver SHA-256 `1ed50fee03be183f1ac2ba2c0b79d1024f7981cc7f0a72f8560957cd66168905` verifies both rule asks and deny have no file effect, ordinary/unchanged/mode cases write their expected bytes, two owned requests per case and released locks. At `2e42a5c978174f1b834b453828c72e26fb176fc001ea8b71711f1c28e801ef95`, inputs `b175059b024a0ff5f7674c3541653c37bc56581064a24cdc4b559b7f0db203c5`, validation `1aee62fa311e55c7e25f297213405734893d75ff98f597bdad5536c6c513f7fb`, build `655f6ed1e72f4bde2b85a293b37c124eeddec0529d51f06bbbe1ff516ca0408a`. Later plan-test-only assertions yield `7e8be1b6` with the same inputs and build. An independent `VERIFIED_SCOPED` review read the cited source bodies and host rule ordering, matched the two reviewed file hashes, ran the two focused groups 2/2 and reran the six-case compiled driver. It did not rehash source modules, rerun build/the 106 tests or exercise classic hook rewrite. Afterward main added a classic plugin-chain regression with actual file effects: a rewritten path matching the explicit ask remains absent and an ordinary rewritten path is written; the classic suite passes 5/5 and check passes at `19ac25020f8ed19fa4c683c73a5851be98a86fb70ae1a21061cc3e8242915da2`, with inputs/build unchanged from `7e8be1b6`. That added test was outside the independent review. A later fixture-only cleanup moves the leaf test's temporary roots to the job scratch when available and registers classic hook-module directories with the existing cleanup owner. Both suites pass 11/11 and check at `843c758b51dae75a769b7f4fb6652b86e03e7f8621386aec091baa28f6c02b08`, validation `609dc3292199f6fabad9eec6b181f6318902218a22051f98b8e2e818f4812b93`, with unchanged inputs/build; the reviewed leaf test's later hash is `3bfe76d863c177c84507aa1341a44d916af3828370c900646701c2218852eb12`. The fixture-only changes were not independently reviewed. Its report identifies a further unhandled source condition: `sxr` rechecks `requiresUserInteraction` even without updated input, while this host rechecks only on updated input. Source `yk` also returns protected interaction (`GAn`), nested rule (`utt`/`m0`), safety (`Dl`) and sandbox asks under its own predicates. Host `PermissionDecision.floor` defines additional variants, but current direct producers shown by the local search emit `rule` and incomplete `readings`; this narrow adapter does not claim to cover the other source categories or platforms. Main independently verified the recorded source module hashes.

Interaction hook follow-up: the earlier blanket statement that an updated-input explicit-rule ask always converts to denial is scoped only to source `sxr`. A bounded independent source reader verified `sxr -> _or -> kpn` in `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` [3317869,3320629), module SHA-256 `4c74137eb23221981404d0ca87b765912f83344851d5c132b21857e5685b03da`; `_or` [3319984,3320240) has body SHA-256 `d4c9fbabfc9124fa614721bbaf238566c1dd6093d390a517f6f732a87a8f5b6d`; `kpn` [3320240,3320629) has `2ea0e60cb2c7f74e46d1dfb2ae9a6900a70435a3f245264905451274b1e96fb2` and selects that branch when `shouldAvoidPermissionPrompts` holds. `kxr`/`Sxr`/`GAn` [3355478,3355651) have combined body SHA-256 `0de57fb851d5ba50287275b95663fd13c7d8267b6e9201e054d6c1eb0c5184ee`. Its non-strict `sxr` rechecks changed input or an interactive tool and converts a returned ask to deny. The reader separately traced interactive `g1t.runHooks`, `chunk-9c5se12s.js` [7100,8340), module SHA-256 `a701e1d055f89288d2387018877f50d06a67ea82c1bc7209a21cf5bf3487c0f2`, body SHA-256 `fc51e34d73263e950e4a7440af1acb4598a219380906aa036de18721f8455a00`: unchanged allow on `requiresUserInteraction` returns null so the dialog continues; changed input reaching a rule ask returns `{reprompted,finalInput}`. Source `Go` invokes `onReprompt` with that input and decision, and `Ie` replaces its displayed input and result before rebuilding the dialog. Main independently read those branches and the `i1t` callers in `chunk-24k7d6mw.js` and `chunk-kwdhpnes.js`; the coordinator `i1t` [4681,5320), body SHA-256 `9386f202ef5ffcf8e2eb650da1d7fe2a3ccfa785d13d9fe1df0017ad57f817da`, may instead discard a reprompt result and continue the original dialog, so its scheduling path must remain distinct. The `Go` reprompt dispatch excerpt [45300,45560) has SHA-256 `f2dc7864d27153735d7455c0ecb77eb6814142076ba2f8a28a4e383c61f3ac4c`, and the `Ie` callback excerpt [53050,53650) has `3eeb53036f5879b66bf76adeb06f95de7f68f8fb9ad88d9064b3127f74929642`; both are excerpt rather than complete-function digests. Source `kxr` contains `ExitPlanMode` and `AskUserQuestion`; on changed input with no `mcpInfo` those names bypass only `GAn`'s interaction ask, not a rule ask. The corresponding host `submit_plan` has no established `kxr` source identity. The reader verified module hashes, source ranges and name bindings without running the source application. The source `ExitPlanMode.requiresUserInteraction` also depends on its team/agent state, whereas the host flag is static. The host's current universal `floor:'rule'` denial matches the observed no-prompt `sxr` path but not the interactive dialog's reprompt path; the 12-case baseline below confirms the current CLI denies a rewritten rule ask even with an interactive callback. The local no-rewrite hook currently reaches a distinct tool interaction rather than the source coordinator's original permission dialog. Host `interactiveLoop` supplies a permission callback, but resident-worker mode also constructs that callback while `readAnswer` rejects terminal input. Headless CLI, registry children and foreground skill agents do not supply it; tool-level question/review callbacks are a separate surface. Callback presence therefore is not by itself a complete prompt-availability test. Source `vC`, `chunk-40cepjpq.js` module SHA-256 `473c9dfb84c9defcb7ca5bd5e6f598cd951274dc2c1334a67c8b9d25b451a8b2`, starts at UTF-16 offset 121081; its observed child-context branch at 124876 sets `awaitAutomatedChecksBeforeDialog` when `isAsync` is true and prompts remain available. `Go` runs hook results concurrently with the dialog only when that flag is false, while the earlier `i1t` coordinator returns null for a reprompt result before the original dialog is created. This is an additional source scheduling boundary, not proof that every host callback has the same mode. A bounded repair must preserve both owned host interaction and explicit rule approval while distinguishing actual prompt-capable and no-prompt owners; the exact dialog/coordinator mode, user edits, cancellation, updates and PermissionRequest re-entry require coverage. A narrow host design for the synchronous local callback is to carry the rewritten input and the new rule-ask reason into one explicit user approval, then continue with that input only after the callback allows; the no-prompt path keeps the current denial. The `PermissionRequest` hook is not rerun by the source `Ie` reprompt callback. For a prompt-capable main session the candidate host state would be one mutable request `{input, reason, signal}` with a boolean answer, while a no-prompt owner retains the current deny; the existing owner API does not expose whether an available callback represents a human dialog or a closed resident input. The host cannot reconstruct the source's concurrent dialog race or its separate `i1t` scheduling through this single callback, so those stay stated differences. Before adopting an answer, check cancellation and re-evaluate any newly imposed deny or changed path observation; do not adopt the hook's proposed permission updates on the reprompt branch. An unchanged interactive-tool allow needs the host's tool-specific question/review callback rather than a generic permission prompt; if that owner is absent, preserve the no-prompt denial. `submit_plan` remains a host-only interaction with no source `kxr` exception, so it needs its own explicit adaptation rather than inheriting a source-name claim. This is a proposed mapping, pending an actual CLI red/green, cancellation and independent review. No existing independent review established the broader interactive equivalence.

The non-baseline `node scripts/smoke-approval-retarget-cli.mjs` was rerun at the unchanged build `655f6ed1` after the interaction baseline and again stops at `read_file/retarget`, where the actual result contains `PROTECTED_FIXTURE` and has no error. Later cases are not reached. A separate `--baseline` run at identity `d49e99c8` again completes all 18 cases: each of the six retarget cases reaches its protected fixture, while its stable and direct-denial controls behave as recorded. The same 18-case baseline was rerun after the interactive candidate at `bf4b93bc`; all six retargets still reach the protected fixture. No approval-to-use repair is claimed.

An owned Agent observation and a compiled inline CLI baseline exercise `ask_user_question`, `submit_plan` and `exit_plan_mode`, each with an explicit whole-tool ask under unchanged hook allow, hook allow with `updatedInput`, manual approval, and no ask rule. For each unchanged allow the real host interaction still appears; the rewritten whole-tool ask is denied before it appears. Manual approval reaches the same interaction, and no-rule calls reach it without a PermissionRequest. The CLI driver checks each prompt, returned answer or plan result, both local model requests, hook marker, process exit and lock release. It uses a loopback fixture and sends no upstream model request. This is a host baseline, not a claim about the source branch. It does not run the classic module through the compiled CLI. Twelve CLI scenarios pass at identity `20a343dfb2358cfccd35e48235cee1017f47689b2b04ff8dbb56b1c836686d09`, inputs `d68dd3fa4544690d9674fb9c5d65574eae97a12b0170276ac5804fbd988f3a16`, validation `609dc3292199f6fabad9eec6b181f6318902218a22051f98b8e2e818f4812b93` and build `655f6ed1e72f4bde2b85a293b37c124eeddec0529d51f06bbbe1ff516ca0408a`. Product TypeScript did not change in this observation. The later independent source result is recorded above. A subsequent driver adds `--desired`: on the unchanged compiled build it fails at rewritten `ask_user_question` because the model receives the rule-denial result before the expected prompt; later desired cases are not reached. The default baseline still passes all twelve cases. The extended driver's SHA-256 is `0393941d0671b1d25e80e2f4c9a4b470d16b5d2dc5415a6cdb866de80e28a0a3`; the earlier `44834fc1` hash names the driver version used for the recorded `20a343df` identity. The extended driver and unchanged build yield identity `d49e99c887598ccb83ab64cc63a1e2b2b99ec6a77d2b6abad3cf0a2cec639e51`, inputs `95fbf30fcf319d68ad837e940201cc081167b63f2fd405e8f7effbf387b7f077`, validation `609dc3292199f6fabad9eec6b181f6318902218a22051f98b8e2e818f4812b93` and build `655f6ed1e72f4bde2b85a293b37c124eeddec0529d51f06bbbe1ff516ca0408a`.

Interactive reprompt candidate: `Agent.authorize` adapts `g1t.runHooks` [7100,8340), with the `Go` and `Ie` dispatch excerpts and module identities in the source record above. For a PermissionRequest allow with rewritten input and an explicit-rule ask, a host marked `permissionPrompt:'interactive'` asks with that input and its new reason; headless and resident owners keep the `sxr` denial. The input snapshot and cancellation are checked around the callback and final clamp. A current deny stops execution; a changed ask bucket, policy or workspace prompts again. Hook-supplied permission updates are not adopted in this branch. `PreToolUse` still receives the approved input and its own rewrite returns through authorization. The source concurrent-dialog and `i1t` paths do not map exactly to this synchronous callback, `submit_plan` is a host adaptation rather than a `kxr` source-name claim, and this change does not bind approval to a file handle.

The added Agent group exercises approval, rejection, cancellation, changed deny, changed ask, changed input, mutation during reprompt, a denied `PreToolUse` rewrite, headless and resident modes. A classic hook group checks approval and rejection with actual file effects. A resident peer test preserves the existing closed-input control and adds a second turn with a rewritten ask; neither file is written. The real compiled inline driver `node scripts/smoke-interaction-permission-cli.mjs --desired` passes 12/12 cases and verifies interaction prompts, outputs, local request counts, process exit and lock release. The three added groups plus related suites pass 86/86; `npm run check`, `npm run build` and `git diff --check` pass. Identity is `bf4b93bcc404ac7d62bf6c806752a4fd6bb303a48286b14830af68fc9185a7dd`, inputs `40fa478dfd845eb6e1ded10741d1f3cce9e264fe6830ce6e9d77ed052f9acaaa`, validation `f895416a88f77e787715be9036e7a1626ba00f349e06acc73ed28b120c671337`, build `83e61bd5dfa4e21662b0a74feb999f2a74d0c5be17fc57cfc5542f29e7c3b42f`. Product `src/agent.ts` is `43e5b416922208ee48d5e0b2c740bcb7306a6df731656d8e11d618859f7a6feb`; `src/interactive.ts` is `d6683c27e350f98a3a7c2a7d35ed96ee570235cf212f00f796230e43dbb72dbe`. The design reader terminated on an API budget error with no final verdict. A separate bounded implementation review returned `NOT_VERIFIED`: it reproduced a HOME mismatch, mutation of a callback-held input reference after approval, headless execution after an ordinary mode ask, and a repeated prompt after identical `PreToolUse.updatedInput`. It reran three focused regressions and the 12-case compiled CLI, without rebuilding. Main reproduced the HOME case with a real Shell tool: a new deny on the HOME bound for execution was checked under the callback's changed HOME, and the denied command wrote a marker. The reprompt now rechecks and reclamps under the execution-bound HOME. Main also reproduced a callback retaining and changing the input during an awaited PreToolUse hook; the callback now receives a structured clone, and both immediate and delayed mutation controls exercise the unchanged approved input. These repairs remain pending full verification and fresh independent review.

The two remaining review observations need source qualification. `yk` [3357795,3359265) and `m0`/`utt` [3354561,3354848), combined SHA-256 `b109fd704b12c35c05608ec15958dd7957ce9ea0961284bd9a155a3cb3851362`, return rule, interactive, safety and sandbox asks, but filter ordinary mode asks before `sxr` sees them; the source alone does not establish that every host `floor:undefined` ask must deny. The host's ordinary-mode fixture is retained, and protected non-rule asks remain open. The source `_Wo` [2237279,2239655), SHA-256 `b5af0ffa2181db2da644e010f38119b64568ad9458c2dc58473cea792004b4d3`, runs `yk` for a hook `allow` or `ask`, then invokes `canUseTool` when that recheck returns ask; it contains no equality exemption. The calling excerpt [2284640,2285000), SHA-256 `793e046ff95d5200416016179d1d1d966c9cfab46e8b462ee47482bc8384898d`, passes the PreToolUse result and input through `Msr`; `Cge` [2239655,2240346), SHA-256 `3679f7e8f5c89849901ba1c25d7a8238c3f20bea0c580bf3c9addc47c87d4ba0`, also invokes `canUseTool` when its evaluated result asks. The calling excerpt is not a complete function range. A host regression reproduces two confirmations and two PermissionRequest events after an identical PreToolUse rewrite, but this source excerpt does not establish all coordinator state or the exact event count. ## Independent source trace

A separate independent reader checked the merge and entry branch without rerunning the sample or recalculating the module digest. With an allow baseline and all sampled `O3e` results also allow, it confirmed `DTr` preserves allow for three exhaustive positions and creates an `other` soft ask for four incomplete positions. `sfn` leaves the ask unchanged when outside-read blocking is off, `jwe` marks it soft, and `ior` changes the original allow into passthrough. Under ordinary mode and without a whole-tool allow, the final `$pn` passthrough conversion produces ask. This reader did not establish the all-allow premise: `D3e`'s `jcn` subcommand results and `vX` path verdict can return before the whole-line allow, and it did not independently close `Rcn`, `gyr`, all `O3e` samples, or `$pn`'s `Npn` and post-check wrappers. A follow-up reader traced `jcn` declarator [3213895,3215588), SHA-256 `880a55c558adf1d0c2fd1fccdf94da8d90a9d5437d67c7bc5332f3ee76b84c86`, and `vX` [3151331,3156825) for the candidate, concluding that both return passthrough under the recorded conditions and that sandbox auto-allow does not intercept these prefixed `export` commands. Its analysis assumes successful parsing, no clamp and `Nwe=null`; it sampled `vX` dependencies rather than closing them and did not fully resolve `K5e` or `Ccn`. Main subsequently read `K5e` [3096696,3097367) and `Ccn` [3196455,3197089): the former only produces its special decision for a recognized `sed` command, and the latter delegates to `Qvr` [3196108,3196455), which returns passthrough in default mode. With the recorded `export` command texts and no deny/ask rules, these two calls preserve the conditional passthrough; this is a targeted source trace, not a complete review of their other inputs. Neither reader ran the original sample or established a complete final verdict for the literal commands.

A bounded reader traced the two literal command trees without executing recovered code. Given the recorded compiled `vS` tree and a successful source parse, the reader found that the ASCII input passes the applicable `E6` prechecks: `He` sees no comments or parentheses, `Ze` accepts the plain `export` operands via `Ie`, and the coverage, redirection and heredoc checks have no matching node. `E` visits each top-level `command`; `Kt` gives `jt` one assignment and `export` plus one identifier. The source `xe`/`ct`/`B9` checks accept the literal names and value. On the first pass `jt` collects `M=[false,false,false,false]` and `L=4`, since each export operand differs from its preceding assignment. `pyr(4,M)` yields six vectors and `exhaustive: false`; the three-prefix case yields eight vectors and `exhaustive: true`. For those vectors the reader traced `jt`'s length and value checks and found the candidate's three or four positions remain aligned. The `O3e`/`D3e` callback and final policy decision were outside that first reader's review; the separate merge review and its remaining premises are recorded above. This is a source trace conditional on the recorded tree and parser success, not a runtime execution of the recovered analyser. `w6` [1300,1631) calls the parser with an abort sentinel and is affected by its finite parse budget. For non-ASCII input the parser node positions are UTF-8 byte offsets; the ASCII candidate happens to have matching byte and UTF-16 offsets.

## Host observation

At local commit `154843e`, an owned compiled CLI probe with default-mode `Bash(printf:*)` deny creates no marker for `printf denied > marker.txt`, but both `env CUSTOM=value printf denied > marker.txt` and `command printf denied > marker.txt` create their marker; `echo allowed > marker.txt` also runs. All four use two requests to an owned loopback gateway and isolated fixture workspaces. This confirms a reachable host content-rule gap, not merely a helper mismatch. With the copied analyser and `gyr` under `sourceGlobRecord:true`, all four inputs produce a simple command and `{ok:true}`. The copied `xX` with a project deny-rule object returns one matching prefix rule for the first three and zero for the echo control. The existing `jcn` fixture returns deny for the first three before its path callback; the echo control reaches the deliberately throwing path sentinel. Passing each analysed command's redirect-free `text` and AST to that fixture also returns the same deny rule. Source `RTr` [3221802,3222145), SHA-256 `358956357221afd4c0fbc15f2770c95318890fafb22a4b6309435933b515dec8`, keeps these non-`cd` command texts paired with their ASTs; `D3e` [3262265,3262627), SHA-256 `c2921337ea1e13a4a3cade22bfe192716a07426292289abd2294950d552e9a17`, returns a subcommand denial when `jcn` denies. `O3e` returns that denial through `$cn`, whose non-ask branch preserves the input; `$pn`'s three post-check wrappers also leave a denial alone before its deny return. The copied analyses have no declaration prefixes. The earlier `x3e` sandbox auto-allow, `Uwe` and `Rcn` early returns remain conditions of the complete source path. Its rule-store, feature and path adapters remain fixture assumptions, and the complete source verdict remains conditional. The CLI probe SHA-256 is `9d5892079fc821c13430417be9aee0b5c596da41cda2e1eb26fa733b87cc8ef8`; it remains in job scratch. Product identity is `1394b93d43197813b87bf8068520a12224af42e640aa44df0e29d8faf259ead4`, inputs `04848f57adccd4563b41c150f292761b5c23153926b4aa02f8ed1780d523a625`, validation `7cf253f5bf59ead40eaec3f98e58aa5053165c45a88203640bd5eb1a3004502e`, build `6dd72e13b88229d254770a657d99b8ced6c87e57374d0843b87161f897938f60`. The pre-change declaration-prefix compiled driver was also rerun at this identity: three and four prefixes ran, the explicit last-command deny blocked, and the later-value command asked. Its revised scratch driver SHA-256 is `cf060ad284a018e83edf0fbd06fc2be67af9bd71fd08b42e384b2357f2b69d2f`.

A temporary owned probe used the compiled product's already-ported `vS` parser and `shellReadings`, not the recovered application. `X=1 declare y`, `X=1 export Y`, `if true; then X=1 export Y; fi`, `X=1 echo ok` and `declare y` all produced `exhaustive: true` on this host. The first three parsed prefixed declarations as `command` nodes with a `variable_assignment`, while the unprefixed declaration parsed as `declaration_command`. The observation establishes host output only; it does not establish how the recovered decision resolves the same inputs. Probe SHA-256 `a824bdaea40becada0763b6555200ca90b789cf7f730802a2c9fbe86a5e7d664`; output SHA-256 `b696da6d36d24761d2451abac8b65cbfe5425b1771b3305769ede3bc8249fb5d`. Both returned `[run_command: exit code 0]`; a third run added a deny rule for `G=1 export H` and returned `Permission to run this command was denied for an alternate reading: run_command`. The seven core `pyr`/`E6`/`Ze`/`Dt`/`E`/`jt`/`Kt` AST bodies span 28,097 UTF-16 units before dependencies. The source walks declarations, wrappers, values and control-flow state before producing its alternate readings. In `jt` [54930,55720), SHA-256 `f1b65e1786f0e855a986e02e5fbfdf5821b0b98b42fd4e72111db615e4fc2339`, a supplied true prefix position attempts a checked variable write, while false skips that write; reaching this block and the eventual result depend on the preceding parser and checks. `Kt` turns the observed assignment node into an env-variable record and the command words into argv before invoking `jt`; `xe` [65986,67564), SHA-256 `52110a65d4e7c628424063ddfa03b9683489d21758f084b15fb2008a44ad9be4`, and `ct` [80313,80477), SHA-256 `cc264d104b5381f0963326b1ce61ca88c3e829739d6b345a93b27c86d06a1cc9`, supply additional refusal checks. A bounded independent reader confirmed this branch but was stopped before returning a complete case verdict; its partial result does not establish a final decision. W373's candidate statement that every such prefixed line should be non-exhaustive is therefore unproven. In particular, one to three boolean readings are exhaustive according to `pyr`; whether a reading is accepted depends on the other recovered analyser checks and permission path. The compiled CLI baseline is recorded above. Exact source dependency closure, final source-policy decisions for concrete cases, and caller adaptation remain necessary before product edits. The recovered application and its original entrypoints are not executed in this workflow; any later probe must run only audited, ported bodies in an owned fixture. The module parses statically with the project's `acorn` dependency. The scope-aware `scripts/source-undeclared-scan.mjs` reports 239 declared names and zero unresolved names for the complete module; this does not establish external-import semantics or an executable dependency closure. Its imports are the parser (`vS`, `hyr`, `yyr`), `wd`, `_s`/`Ujn`/`$Ts`, telemetry `i`, and Node `os.homedir`. The scan retains the four original Bun module paths. Three dependency files match their manifest digests: parser `chunk-y59c9wzk.js` `6b51509a24e12a727cdf7e99b8a6e7e04921ea9b411eeeb0275d19db960a5ae2`, `chunk-j41p6s5m.js` `b5b2bc8fd8ceb2339e3d4abcb5f3548e896ee99b9889591bd14e492e12225856`, and `chunk-0yczyn9c.js` `9dc8063ec0affdd72ca6fddf4f306a1cdeed8672a773870076125c478fadaaea`; telemetry module `chunk-4nygtnjw.js` also matches manifest digest `c89ccc5e465039bc6db9bccec834cf70f485266ae17f2eaf0f9ce72adb69ebc3`; its `i` [1290,1407), body SHA-256 `7f2df95e7a21ebb980f33de170d716031fa6e71b459046f55a71def4a55e662f`, writes to an installed sink or queues the event; its initialization and sink dependencies remain unreviewed. Parser helpers `hyr` [976,1094) and `yyr` [7987,8090) are present as exact source substrings but are not exported by `src/bash-parser-source.ts`; their source SHA-256s are `24b74293cc36ef7497fc49a9ece35f98e517b49e0cf6089ff64caa7544a962bc` and `25b4598ef0ba235c21de1001cfcd9c30c97b30b2f0e448d18fa5e8d1aa82185d`. Static `acorn` inspection found five imports, 98 function declarations, 29 variable declarations and one export at module top level; a static walk of direct initializer calls found `Symbol`, `String.fromCharCode` and `z.source.slice`; `z` is the internal identifier regex in [17909,18194). An AST walk of `E6` reports direct identifier calls `Be`, `Dt`, `Fe`, `Gt`, `He`, `Ue`, `Ze`, `Zt`, `nt`, `te`, `tt`, `we` and local helper `a`; local helper `l` is called inside `a`. A naive direct-call census adds 8 identifiers for `Ze`, 3 for `He`, 15 for `Kt`, 4 for `jt` and 25 for `E`; these counts include local helpers and recursion, and are not a resolved dependency graph.
